13	
14	const storedTheme = localStorage.getItem('theme');
15	
16	// Follow operating system theme preference
17	if (storedTheme === null && window.matchMedia) {
18	const mq = window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)');
19	if (mq.matches) {
20	document.documentElement.classList.add('dark-mode');
21	}
22	} else if (storedTheme === 'dark') {
23	document.documentElement.classList.add('dark-mode');
24	}

1066	
1067	File system#
1068	
1069	Stability: 2 - Stable
1070	
1071	Source Code: lib/fs.js
1072	The node:fs module enables interacting with the file system in a
1073	way modeled on standard POSIX functions.
1074	To use the promise-based APIs:
1075	
1076	import * as fs from 'node:fs/promises';const fs = require('node:fs/promises');copy
1077	To use the callback and sync APIs:
1078	
1079	import * as fs from 'node:fs';const fs = require('node:fs');copy
1080	All file system operations have synchronous, callback, and promise-based
1081	forms, and are accessible using both CommonJS syntax and ES6 Modules (ESM).
1082	Promise example#
1083	Promise-based operations return a promise that is fulfilled when the
1084	asynchronous operation is complete.
1085	
1086	import { unlink } from 'node:fs/promises';
1087	
1088	try {
1089	await unlink('/tmp/hello');
1090	console.log('successfully deleted /tmp/hello');
1091	} catch (error) {
1092	console.error('there was an error:', error.message);
1093	}const { unlink } = require('node:fs/promises');
1094	
1095	(async function(path) {
1096	try {
1097	await unlink(path);
1098	console.log(`successfully deleted ${path}`);
1099	} catch (error) {
1100	console.error('there was an error:', error.message);
1101	}
1102	})('/tmp/hello');copy
1103	Callback example#
1104	The callback form takes a completion callback function as its last
1105	argument and invokes the operation asynchronously. The arguments passed to
1106	the completion callback depend on the method, but the first argument is always
1107	reserved for an exception. If the operation is completed successfully, then
1108	the first argument is null or undefined.
1109	
1110	import { unlink } from 'node:fs';
1111	
1112	unlink('/tmp/hello', (err) => {
1113	if (err) throw err;
1114	console.log('successfully deleted /tmp/hello');
1115	});const { unlink } = require('node:fs');
1116	
1117	unlink('/tmp/hello', (err) => {
1118	if (err) throw err;
1119	console.log('successfully deleted /tmp/hello');
1120	});copy
1121	The callback-based versions of the node:fs module APIs are preferable over
1122	the use of the promise APIs when maximal performance (both in terms of
1123	execution time and memory allocation) is required.
1124	Synchronous example#
1125	The synchronous APIs block the Node.js event loop and further JavaScript
1126	execution until the operation is complete. Exceptions are thrown immediately
1127	and can be handled using try…catch, or can be allowed to bubble up.
1128	
1129	import { unlinkSync } from 'node:fs';
1130	
1131	try {
1132	unlinkSync('/tmp/hello');
1133	console.log('successfully deleted /tmp/hello');
1134	} catch (err) {
1135	// handle the error
1136	}const { unlinkSync } = require('node:fs');
1137	
1138	try {
1139	unlinkSync('/tmp/hello');
1140	console.log('successfully deleted /tmp/hello');
1141	} catch (err) {
1142	// handle the error
1143	}copy
1144	Promises API#
1145	
1146	History
1147	
1148	VersionChanges
1149	v14.0.0
1150	Exposed as require('fs/promises').
1151	v11.14.0, v10.17.0
1152	This API is no longer experimental.
1153	v10.1.0
1154	The API is accessible via require('fs').promises only.
1155	v10.0.0
1156	Added in: v10.0.0
1157	
1158	
1159	
1160	The fs/promises API provides asynchronous file system methods that return
1161	promises.
1162	The promise APIs use the underlying Node.js threadpool to perform file
1163	system operations off the event loop thread. These operations are not
1164	synchronized or threadsafe. Care must be taken when performing multiple
1165	concurrent modifications on the same file or data corruption may occur.
1166	
1167	Class: FileHandle#
1168	
1169	Added in: v10.0.0
1170	
1171	A <FileHandle> object is an object wrapper for a numeric file descriptor.
1172	Instances of the <FileHandle> object are created by the fsPromises.open()
1173	method.
1174	All <FileHandle> objects are <EventEmitter>s.
1175	If a <FileHandle> is not closed using the filehandle.close() method, it will
1176	try to automatically close the file descriptor and emit a process warning,
1177	helping to prevent memory leaks. Please do not rely on this behavior because
1178	it can be unreliable and the file may not be closed. Instead, always explicitly
1179	close <FileHandle>s. Node.js may change this behavior in the future.
1180	
1181	Event: 'close'#
1182	
1183	Added in: v15.4.0
1184	
1185	The 'close' event is emitted when the <FileHandle> has been closed and can no
1186	longer be used.
1187	
1188	filehandle.appendFile(data[, options])#
1189	
1190	History
1191	
1192	VersionChanges
1193	v21.1.0, v20.10.0
1194	The flush option is now supported.
1195	v15.14.0, v14.18.0
1196	The data argument supports AsyncIterable, Iterable, and Stream.
1197	v14.0.0
1198	The data parameter won't coerce unsupported input to strings anymore.
1199	v10.0.0
1200	Added in: v10.0.0
1201	
1202	
1203	
1204	
1205	data <string> | <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> | <AsyncIterable> | <Iterable>
1206	options <Object> | <string>
1207	
1208	encoding <string> | <null> Default: 'utf8'
1209	signal <AbortSignal> | <undefined> allows aborting an in-progress writeFile. Default: undefined
1210	
1211	
1212	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
1213	
1214	Alias of filehandle.writeFile().
1215	When operating on file handles, the mode cannot be changed from what it was set
1216	to with fsPromises.open(). Therefore, this is equivalent to
1217	filehandle.writeFile().
1218	
1219	filehandle.chmod(mode)#

1225	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
1226	
1227	Modifies the permissions on the file. See chmod(2).
1228	
1229	filehandle.chown(uid, gid)#
1230	
1231	Added in: v10.0.0
1232	
1233	
1234	uid <integer> The file's new owner's user id.
1235	gid <integer> The file's new group's group id.
1236	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
1237	
1238	Changes the ownership of the file. A wrapper for chown(2).
1239	
1240	filehandle.close()#
1241	
1242	Added in: v10.0.0
1243	
1244	
1245	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
1246	
1247	Closes the file handle after waiting for any pending operation on the handle to
1248	complete.
1249	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
1250	
1251	let filehandle;
1252	try {
1253	filehandle = await open('thefile.txt', 'r');
1254	} finally {
1255	await filehandle?.close();
1256	} copy
1257	
1258	filehandle.createReadStream([options])#
1259	
1260	Added in: v16.11.0
1261	
1262	
1263	options <Object>
1264	
1265	encoding <string> Default: null
1266	autoClose <boolean> Default: true
1267	emitClose <boolean> Default: true
1268	start <integer>
1269	end <integer> Default: Infinity
1270	highWaterMark <integer> Default: 64 * 1024
1271	signal <AbortSignal> | <undefined> Default: undefined
1272	
1273	
1274	Returns: <fs.ReadStream>
1275	
1276	options can include start and end values to read a range of bytes from
1277	the file instead of the entire file. Both start and end are inclusive and
1278	start counting at 0, allowed values are in the
1279	[0, Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER] range. If start is
1280	omitted or undefined, filehandle.createReadStream() reads sequentially from
1281	the current file position. The encoding can be any one of those accepted by
1282	<Buffer>.
1283	If the FileHandle points to a character device that only supports blocking
1284	reads (such as keyboard or sound card), read operations do not finish until data
1285	is available. This can prevent the process from exiting and the stream from
1286	closing naturally.
1287	By default, the stream will emit a 'close' event after it has been
1288	destroyed. Set the emitClose option to false to change this behavior.
1289	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
1290	
1291	const fd = await open('/dev/input/event0');
1292	// Create a stream from some character device.
1293	const stream = fd.createReadStream();
1294	setTimeout(() => {
1295	stream.close(); // This may not close the stream.
1296	// Artificially marking end-of-stream, as if the underlying resource had
1297	// indicated end-of-file by itself, allows the stream to close.
1298	// This does not cancel pending read operations, and if there is such an
1299	// operation, the process may still not be able to exit successfully
1300	// until it finishes.
1301	stream.push(null);
1302	stream.read(0);
1303	}, 100); copy
1304	If autoClose is false, then the file descriptor won't be closed, even if
1305	there's an error. It is the application's responsibility to close it and make
1306	sure there's no file descriptor leak. If autoClose is set to true (default
1307	behavior), on 'error' or 'end' the file descriptor will be closed
1308	automatically.
1309	An example to read the last 10 bytes of a file which is 100 bytes long:
1310	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
1311	
1312	const fd = await open('sample.txt');
1313	fd.createReadStream({ start: 90, end: 99 }); copy
1314	
1315	filehandle.createWriteStream([options])#
1316	
1317	History
1318	
1319	VersionChanges
1320	v22.0.0
1321	bump default highWaterMark.
1322	v21.0.0, v20.10.0
1323	The flush option is now supported.
1324	v16.11.0
1325	Added in: v16.11.0
1326	
1327	
1328	
1329	
1330	options <Object>
1331	
1332	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
1333	autoClose <boolean> Default: true
1334	emitClose <boolean> Default: true
1335	start <integer>
1336	highWaterMark <number> Default: See
1337	stream.getDefaultHighWaterMark().
1338	flush <boolean> If true, the underlying file descriptor is flushed
1339	prior to closing it. Default: false.
1340	
1341	
1342	Returns: <fs.WriteStream>
1343	
1344	options may also include a start option to allow writing data at some
1345	position past the beginning of the file, allowed values are in the
1346	[0, Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER] range. Modifying a file rather than
1347	replacing it may require the flags open option to be set to r+ rather than
1348	the default r. The encoding can be any one of those accepted by <Buffer>.
1349	If autoClose is set to true (default behavior) on 'error' or 'finish'
1350	the file descriptor will be closed automatically. If autoClose is false,
1351	then the file descriptor won't be closed, even if there's an error.
1352	It is the application's responsibility to close it and make sure there's no
1353	file descriptor leak.
1354	By default, the stream will emit a 'close' event after it has been
1355	destroyed. Set the emitClose option to false to change this behavior.
1356	
1357	filehandle.datasync()#
1358	
1359	Added in: v10.0.0
1360	
1361	
1362	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
1363	
1364	Forces all currently queued I/O operations associated with the file to the
1365	operating system's synchronized I/O completion state. Refer to the POSIX
1366	fdatasync(2) documentation for details.
1367	Unlike filehandle.sync this method does not flush modified metadata.
1368

1371	Added in: v10.0.0
1372	
1373	
1374	Type: <number> The numeric file descriptor managed by the <FileHandle> object.
1375	
1376	
1377	filehandle.pull([...transforms][, options])#
1378	
1379	Added in: v24.20.0
1380	
1381	Stability: 1 - Experimental
1382	
1383	...transforms <Function> | <Object> Optional transforms to apply via
1384	stream/iter pull().
1385	options <Object>
1386	
1387	signal <AbortSignal>
1388	autoClose <boolean> Close the file handle when the stream ends.
1389	Default: false.
1390	start <number> Byte offset to begin reading from. When specified,
1391	reads use explicit positioning (pread semantics). Default: current
1392	file position.
1393	limit <number> Maximum number of bytes to read before ending the
1394	iterator. Reads stop when limit bytes have been delivered or EOF is
1395	reached, whichever comes first. Default: read until EOF.
1396	chunkSize <number> Size in bytes of the buffer allocated for each
1397	read operation. Default: 131072 (128 KB).
1398	
1399	
1400	Returns: <AsyncIterable> whose chunks fulfill with <Uint8Array[]>
1401	
1402	Return the file contents as an async iterable using the
1403	node:stream/iter pull model. Reads are performed in chunkSize-byte
1404	chunks (default 128 KB). If transforms are provided, they are applied
1405	via stream/iter pull().
1406	The file handle is locked while the iterable is being consumed and unlocked
1407	when iteration completes, an error occurs, or the consumer breaks.
1408	This function is only available when the --experimental-stream-iter flag is
1409	enabled.
1410	
1411	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
1412	import { text } from 'node:stream/iter';
1413	import { compressGzip } from 'node:zlib/iter';
1414	
1415	const fh = await open('input.txt', 'r');
1416	
1417	// Read as text
1418	console.log(await text(fh.pull({ autoClose: true })));
1419	
1420	// Read 1 KB starting at byte 100
1421	const fh2 = await open('input.txt', 'r');
1422	console.log(await text(fh2.pull({ start: 100, limit: 1024, autoClose: true })));
1423	
1424	// Read with compression
1425	const fh3 = await open('input.txt', 'r');
1426	const compressed = fh3.pull(compressGzip(), { autoClose: true });const { open } = require('node:fs/promises');
1427	const { text } = require('node:stream/iter');
1428	const { compressGzip } = require('node:zlib/iter');
1429	
1430	async function run() {
1431	const fh = await open('input.txt', 'r');
1432	
1433	// Read as text
1434	console.log(await text(fh.pull({ autoClose: true })));
1435	
1436	// Read 1 KB starting at byte 100
1437	const fh2 = await open('input.txt', 'r');
1438	console.log(await text(fh2.pull({ start: 100, limit: 1024, autoClose: true })));
1439	
1440	// Read with compression
1441	const fh3 = await open('input.txt', 'r');
1442	const compressed = fh3.pull(compressGzip(), { autoClose: true });
1443	}
1444	
1445	run().catch(console.error);copy
1446	
1447	filehandle.pullSync([...transforms][, options])#
1448	
1449	Added in: v24.20.0
1450	
1451	Stability: 1 - Experimental
1452	
1453	...transforms <Function> | <Object> Optional transforms to apply via
1454	stream/iter pullSync().
1455	options <Object>
1456	
1457	autoClose <boolean> Close the file handle when the stream ends.
1458	Default: false.
1459	start <number> Byte offset to begin reading from. When specified,
1460	reads use explicit positioning. Default: current file position.
1461	limit <number> Maximum number of bytes to read before ending the
1462	iterator. Default: read until EOF.
1463	chunkSize <number> Size in bytes of the buffer allocated for each
1464	read operation. Default: 131072 (128 KB).
1465	
1466	
1467	Returns: <Iterable> whose chunks return <Uint8Array[]>
1468	
1469	Synchronous counterpart of filehandle.pull(). Returns a sync iterable
1470	that reads the file using synchronous I/O on the main thread. Reads are
1471	performed in chunkSize-byte chunks (default 128 KB).
1472	The file handle is locked while the iterable is being consumed. Unlike the
1473	async pull(), this method does not support AbortSignal since all
1474	operations are synchronous.
1475	This function is only available when the --experimental-stream-iter flag is
1476	enabled.
1477	
1478	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
1479	import { textSync, pipeToSync } from 'node:stream/iter';
1480	import { compressGzipSync, decompressGzipSync } from 'node:zlib/iter';
1481	
1482	const fh = await open('input.txt', 'r');
1483	
1484	// Read as text (sync)
1485	console.log(textSync(fh.pullSync({ autoClose: true })));
1486	
1487	// Sync compress pipeline: file -> gzip -> file
1488	const src = await open('input.txt', 'r');
1489	const dst = await open('output.gz', 'w');
1490	pipeToSync(src.pullSync(compressGzipSync(), { autoClose: true }), dst.writer({ autoClose: true }));const { open } = require('node:fs/promises');
1491	const { textSync, pipeToSync } = require('node:stream/iter');
1492	const { compressGzipSync, decompressGzipSync } = require('node:zlib/iter');
1493	
1494	async function run() {
1495	const fh = await open('input.txt', 'r');
1496	
1497	// Read as text (sync)
1498	console.log(textSync(fh.pullSync({ autoClose: true })));
1499	
1500	// Sync compress pipeline: file -> gzip -> file
1501	const src = await open('input.txt', 'r');
1502	const dst = await open('output.gz', 'w');
1503	pipeToSync(
1504	src.pullSync(compressGzipSync(), { autoClose: true }),
1505	dst.writer({ autoClose: true }),
1506	);
1507	}
1508	
1509	run().catch(console.error);copy

1641	Added in: v17.0.0
1642	
1643	
1644	
1645	
1646	options <Object>
1647	
1648	autoClose <boolean> When true, causes the <FileHandle> to be closed when the
1649	stream is closed. Default: false
1650	
1651	
1652	Returns: <ReadableStream>
1653	
1654	Returns a byte-oriented ReadableStream that may be used to read the file's
1655	contents.
1656	An error will be thrown if this method is called more than once or is called
1657	after the FileHandle is closed or closing.
1658

1661	} from 'node:fs/promises';

1664

1667	
1668	await file.close();const {
1669	open,
1670	} = require('node:fs/promises');
1671	
1672	(async () => {
1673	const file = await open('./some/file/to/read');
1674	
1675	for await (const chunk of file.readableWebStream())
1676	console.log(chunk);
1677	
1678	await file.close();
1679	})();copy
1680	While the ReadableStream will read the file to completion, it will not
1681	close the FileHandle automatically. User code must still call the
1682	fileHandle.close() method.

1684	filehandle.readFile(options)#
1685	
1686	History
1687	
1688	VersionChanges
1689	v24.19.0
1690	Added support for the buffer option.
1691	v10.0.0
1692	Added in: v10.0.0
1693	
1694	
1695	
1696	
1697	options <Object> | <string>
1698	
1699	encoding <string> | <null> Default: null
1700	signal <AbortSignal> allows aborting an in-progress readFile
1701	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> | <Function> A buffer to read into, or a
1702	function called with the file size that returns the buffer.
1703	
1704	
1705	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills upon a successful read with the contents of the
1706	file. If no encoding is specified (using options.encoding), the data is
1707	returned as a <Buffer> object. Otherwise, the data will be a string.
1708	
1709	Asynchronously reads the entire contents of a file.
1710	If options is a string, then it specifies the encoding.
1711	If buffer is provided and no encoding is specified, the returned <Buffer> is
1712	a view over the supplied buffer containing only the bytes read. If the
1713	supplied buffer is too small to contain the entire file, the operation will
1714	fail.
1715	The <FileHandle> has to support reading.
1716	If one or more filehandle.read() calls are made on a file handle and then a
1717	filehandle.readFile() call is made, the data will be read from the current
1718	position till the end of the file. It doesn't always read from the beginning
1719	of the file.
1720	An example using the buffer option with a pre-allocated buffer:
1721	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
1722	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
1723	
1724	const file = await open('./some/file/to/read');
1725	try {
1726	const buf = Buffer.alloc(16384);
1727	const contents = await file.readFile({ buffer: buf });
1728	console.log(contents); // A view over `buf` containing only the bytes read
1729	} finally {
1730	await file.close();
1731	} copy
1732	An example using the buffer option with a function returning a buffer:
1733	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
1734	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
1735	
1736	const file = await open('./some/file/to/read');
1737	try {
1738	const contents = await file.readFile({
1739	buffer: (size) => Buffer.alloc(size),
1740	});
1741	console.log(contents);
1742	} finally {
1743	await file.close();
1744	} copy

1836	filehandle.truncate(len)#
1837	
1838	Added in: v10.0.0
1839	
1840	
1841	len <integer> Default: 0
1842	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
1843	
1844	Truncates the file.
1845	If the file was larger than len bytes, only the first len bytes will be
1846	retained in the file.
1847	The following example retains only the first four bytes of the file:
1848	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
1849	
1850	let filehandle = null;
1851	try {
1852	filehandle = await open('temp.txt', 'r+');
1853	await filehandle.truncate(4);
1854	} finally {
1855	await filehandle?.close();
1856	} copy
1857	If the file previously was shorter than len bytes, it is extended, and the
1858	extended part is filled with null bytes ('\0'):
1859	If len is negative then 0 will be used.

1873	filehandle.write(buffer, offset[, length[, position]])#
1874	
1875	History
1876	
1877	VersionChanges
1878	v14.0.0
1879	The buffer parameter won't coerce unsupported input to buffers anymore.
1880	v10.0.0
1881	Added in: v10.0.0
1882	
1883	
1884	
1885	
1886	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView>
1887	offset <integer> The start position from within buffer where the data
1888	to write begins.
1889	length <integer> The number of bytes from buffer to write. Default:
1890	buffer.byteLength - offset
1891	position <integer> | <null> The offset from the beginning of the file where the
1892	data from buffer should be written. If position is not a number,
1893	the data will be written at the current position. See the POSIX pwrite(2)
1894	documentation for more detail. Default: null
1895	Returns: <Promise>
1896	
1897	Write buffer to the file.
1898	The promise is fulfilled with an object containing two properties:
1899	
1900	bytesWritten <integer> the number of bytes written
1901	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> a reference to the
1902	buffer written.
1903	
1904	It is unsafe to use filehandle.write() multiple times on the same file
1905	without waiting for the promise to be fulfilled (or rejected). For this
1906	scenario, use filehandle.createWriteStream().
1907	On Linux, positional writes do not work when the file is opened in append mode.
1908	The kernel ignores the position argument and always appends the data to
1909	the end of the file.

1966	filehandle.writeFile(data, options)#
1967	
1968	History
1969	
1970	VersionChanges
1971	v15.14.0, v14.18.0
1972	The data argument supports AsyncIterable, Iterable, and Stream.
1973	v14.0.0
1974	The data parameter won't coerce unsupported input to strings anymore.
1975	v10.0.0
1976	Added in: v10.0.0
1977	
1978	
1979	
1980	
1981	data <string> | <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> | <AsyncIterable> | <Iterable>
1982	options <Object> | <string>
1983	
1984	encoding <string> | <null> The expected character encoding when data is a
1985	string. Default: 'utf8'
1986	signal <AbortSignal> | <undefined> allows aborting an in-progress writeFile. Default: undefined
1987	
1988	
1989	Returns: <Promise>
1990	
1991	Asynchronously writes data to a file, replacing the file if it already exists.
1992	data can be a string, a buffer, an <AsyncIterable>, or an <Iterable> object.
1993	The promise is fulfilled with no arguments upon success.
1994	If options is a string, then it specifies the encoding.
1995	The <FileHandle> has to support writing.
1996	It is unsafe to use filehandle.writeFile() multiple times on the same file
1997	without waiting for the promise to be fulfilled (or rejected).
1998	If one or more filehandle.write() calls are made on a file handle and then a
1999	filehandle.writeFile() call is made, the data will be written from the
2000	current position till the end of the file. It doesn't always write from the
2001	beginning of the file.

2026	
2027	filehandle.writer([options])#
2028	
2029	Added in: v24.20.0
2030	
2031	Stability: 1 - Experimental
2032	
2033	options <Object>
2034	
2035	autoClose <boolean> Close the file handle when the writer ends or
2036	fails. Default: false.
2037	start <number> Byte offset to start writing at. When specified,
2038	writes use explicit positioning. Default: current file position.
2039	limit <number> Maximum number of bytes the writer will accept.
2040	Async writes (write(), writev()) that would exceed the limit reject
2041	with ERR_OUT_OF_RANGE. Sync writes (writeSync(), writevSync())
2042	return false. Default: no limit.
2043	chunkSize <number> Maximum chunk size in bytes for synchronous write
2044	operations. Writes larger than this threshold fall back to async I/O.
2045	Set this to match the reader's chunkSize for optimal pipeTo()
2046	performance. Default: 131072 (128 KB).
2047	
2048	
2049	Returns: <Object>
2050	
2051	write(chunk[, options]) <Function> Returns <Promise>.
2052	Accepts Uint8Array, Buffer, or string (UTF-8 encoded).
2053	
2054	chunk <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> | <string>
2055	options <Object>
2056	
2057	signal <AbortSignal> If the signal is already aborted, the write
2058	rejects with AbortError without performing I/O.
2059	
2060	
2061	
2062	
2063	writev(chunks[, options]) <Function> Returns <Promise>. Uses
2064	scatter/gather I/O via a single writev() syscall. Accepts mixed
2065	Uint8Array/string arrays.
2066	
2067	chunks <Buffer[]> | <TypedArray[]> | <DataView[]> | <string[]>
2068	options <Object>
2069	
2070	signal <AbortSignal> If the signal is already aborted, the write
2071	rejects with AbortError without performing I/O.
2072	
2073	
2074	
2075	
2076	writeSync(chunk) <Function> Returns <boolean>. Attempts a synchronous
2077	write. Returns true if the write succeeded, false if the caller
2078	should fall back to async write(). Returns false when: the writer
2079	is closed/errored, an async operation is in flight, the chunk exceeds
2080	chunkSize, or the write would exceed limit.
2081	
2082	chunk <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> | <string>
2083	
2084	
2085	writevSync(chunks) <Function> Returns <boolean>. Synchronous batch
2086	write. Same fallback semantics as writeSync().
2087	
2088	chunks <Buffer[]> | <TypedArray[]> | <DataView[]> | <string[]>
2089	
2090	
2091	end([options]) <Function> Returns <Promise>, fulfills with the total
2092	number of bytes written. Idempotent: returns totalBytesWritten if already
2093	closed, returns the pending promise if already closing. Rejects if the writer
2094	is in an errored state.
2095	
2096	options <Object>
2097	
2098	signal <AbortSignal> If the signal is already aborted, end()
2099	rejects with AbortError and the writer remains open.
2100	
2101	
2102	
2103	
2104	endSync() <Function> Returns <number> | <number> total bytes written on
2105	success, -1 if the writer is errored or an async operation is in
2106	flight. Idempotent when already closed.
2107	fail(reason) <Function> Puts the writer into a terminal error state.
2108	Synchronous. If the writer is already closed or errored, this is a
2109	no-op. If autoClose is true, closes the file handle synchronously.
2110	
2111	
2112	
2113	Return a node:stream/iter writer backed by this file handle.
2114	The writer supports both Symbol.asyncDispose and Symbol.dispose:
2115	
2116	await using w = fh.writer() — if the writer is still open (no end()
2117	called), asyncDispose calls fail(). If end() is pending, it waits
2118	for it to complete.
2119	using w = fh.writer() — calls fail() unconditionally.
2120	
2121	The writeSync() and writevSync() methods enable the try-sync fast path
2122	used by stream/iter pipeTo(). When the reader's chunk size matches the
2123	writer's chunkSize, all writes in a pipeTo() pipeline complete
2124	synchronously with zero promise overhead.
2125	This function is only available when the --experimental-stream-iter flag is
2126	enabled.
2127	
2128	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
2129	import { from, pipeTo } from 'node:stream/iter';
2130	import { compressGzip } from 'node:zlib/iter';
2131	
2132	// Async pipeline
2133	const fh = await open('output.gz', 'w');
2134	await pipeTo(from('Hello!'), compressGzip(), fh.writer({ autoClose: true }));
2135	
2136	// Sync pipeline with limit
2137	const src = await open('input.txt', 'r');
2138	const dst = await open('output.txt', 'w');
2139	const w = dst.writer({ limit: 1024 * 1024 }); // Max 1 MB
2140	await pipeTo(src.pull({ autoClose: true }), w);
2141	await w.end();
2142	await dst.close();const { open } = require('node:fs/promises');
2143	const { from, pipeTo } = require('node:stream/iter');
2144	const { compressGzip } = require('node:zlib/iter');
2145	
2146	async function run() {
2147	// Async pipeline
2148	const fh = await open('output.gz', 'w');
2149	await pipeTo(from('Hello!'), compressGzip(), fh.writer({ autoClose: true }));
2150	
2151	// Sync pipeline with limit
2152	const src = await open('input.txt', 'r');
2153	const dst = await open('output.txt', 'w');
2154	const w = dst.writer({ limit: 1024 * 1024 }); // Max 1 MB
2155	await pipeTo(src.pull({ autoClose: true }), w);
2156	await w.end();
2157	await dst.close();
2158	}
2159	
2160	run().catch(console.error);copy
2161	
2162	filehandle[Symbol.asyncDispose]()#
2163	
2164	History
2165	
2166	VersionChanges
2167	v24.2.0
2168	No longer experimental.
2169	v20.4.0, v18.18.0
2170	Added in: v20.4.0, v18.18.0
2171	
2172	
2173	
2174	
2175	Returns: <Promise>
2176	
2177	Calls filehandle.close() and returns a promise that fulfills when the
2178	filehandle is closed.
2179	This method enables the filehandle to be used with await using, which
2180	will automatically close the file when the scope exits. For more information,

2182	
2183	fsPromises.access(path[, mode])#
2184	
2185	Added in: v10.0.0
2186	
2187	
2188	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
2189	mode <integer> Default: fs.constants.F_OK
2190	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
2191	
2192	Tests a user's permissions for the file or directory specified by path.
2193	The mode argument is an optional integer that specifies the accessibility
2194	checks to be performed. mode should be either the value fs.constants.F_OK
2195	or a mask consisting of the bitwise OR of any of fs.constants.R_OK,
2196	fs.constants.W_OK, and fs.constants.X_OK (e.g.
2197	fs.constants.W_OK | fs.constants.R_OK). Check File access constants for
2198	possible values of mode.
2199	If the accessibility check is successful, the promise is fulfilled with no
2200	value. If any of the accessibility checks fail, the promise is rejected
2201	with an <Error> object. The following example checks if the file
2202	/etc/passwd can be read and written by the current process.
2203	import { access, constants } from 'node:fs/promises';
2204	
2205	try {
2206	await access('/etc/passwd', constants.R_OK | constants.W_OK);
2207	console.log('can access');
2208	} catch {
2209	console.error('cannot access');
2210	} copy
2211	Using fsPromises.access() to check for the accessibility of a file before
2212	calling fsPromises.open() is not recommended. Doing so introduces a race
2213	condition, since other processes may change the file's state between the two
2214	calls. Instead, user code should open/read/write the file directly and handle
2215	the error raised if the file is not accessible.
2216

2234	options <Object> | <string>
2235

2240	prior to closing it. Default: false.
2241

2244	
2245	Asynchronously append data to a file, creating the file if it does not yet

2276	fsPromises.copyFile(src, dest[, mode])#
2277	
2278	History
2279	
2280	VersionChanges
2281	v14.0.0
2282	Changed flags argument to mode and imposed stricter type validation.
2283	v10.0.0
2284	Added in: v10.0.0
2285	
2286	
2287	
2288	
2289	src <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> source filename to copy
2290	dest <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> destination filename of the copy operation
2291	mode <integer> Optional modifiers that specify the behavior of the copy
2292	operation. It is possible to create a mask consisting of the bitwise OR of
2293	two or more values (e.g.
2294	fs.constants.COPYFILE_EXCL | fs.constants.COPYFILE_FICLONE)
2295	Default: 0.
2296	
2297	fs.constants.COPYFILE_EXCL: The copy operation will fail if dest
2298	already exists.
2299	fs.constants.COPYFILE_FICLONE: The copy operation will attempt to create
2300	a copy-on-write reflink. If the platform does not support copy-on-write,
2301	then a fallback copy mechanism is used.
2302	fs.constants.COPYFILE_FICLONE_FORCE: The copy operation will attempt to
2303	create a copy-on-write reflink. If the platform does not support
2304	copy-on-write, then the operation will fail.
2305	
2306	
2307	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
2308	
2309	Asynchronously copies src to dest. By default, dest is overwritten if it
2310	already exists.
2311	Symbolic links are followed. If src is a symbolic link, the target file is
2312	copied. If dest is a symbolic link, the target file is overwritten unless
2313	mode contains fs.constants.COPYFILE_EXCL.
2314	No guarantees are made about the atomicity of the copy operation. If an
2315	error occurs after the destination file has been opened for writing, an attempt
2316	will be made to remove the destination.
2317	import { copyFile, constants } from 'node:fs/promises';
2318	
2319	try {
2320	await copyFile('source.txt', 'destination.txt');
2321	console.log('source.txt was copied to destination.txt');
2322	} catch {
2323	console.error('The file could not be copied');
2324	}
2325	
2326	// By using COPYFILE_EXCL, the operation will fail if destination.txt exists.
2327	try {
2328	await copyFile('source.txt', 'destination.txt', constants.COPYFILE_EXCL);
2329	console.log('source.txt was copied to destination.txt');
2330	} catch {
2331	console.error('The file could not be copied');
2332	} copy

2335	
2336	History
2337	
2338	VersionChanges
2339	v22.3.0
2340	This API is no longer experimental.
2341	v20.1.0, v18.17.0
2342	Accept an additional mode option to specify the copy behavior as the mode argument of fs.copyFile().
2343	v17.6.0, v16.15.0
2344	Accepts an additional verbatimSymlinks option to specify whether to perform path resolution for symlinks.
2345	v16.7.0
2346	Added in: v16.7.0
2347	
2348	
2349	
2350	
2351	src <string> | <URL> source path to copy.
2352	dest <string> | <URL> destination path to copy to.
2353	options <Object>
2354	
2355	dereference <boolean> dereference symlinks. Default: false.
2356	errorOnExist <boolean> when force is false, and the destination
2357	exists, throw an error. Default: false.
2358	filter <Function> Function to filter copied files/directories. Return
2359	true to copy the item, false to ignore it. When ignoring a directory,
2360	all of its contents will be skipped as well. Can also return a Promise
2361	that resolves to true or false Default: undefined.
2362	
2363	src <string> source path to copy.
2364	dest <string> destination path to copy to.
2365	Returns: <boolean> | <Promise> A value that is coercible to boolean or
2366	a Promise that fulfils with such value.
2367	
2368	
2369	force <boolean> overwrite existing file or directory. The copy
2370	operation will ignore errors if you set this to false and the destination
2371	exists. Use the errorOnExist option to change this behavior.
2372	Default: true.
2373	mode <integer> modifiers for copy operation. Default: 0.
2374	See mode flag of fsPromises.copyFile().
2375	preserveTimestamps <boolean> When true timestamps from src will
2376	be preserved. Default: false.
2377	recursive <boolean> copy directories recursively Default: false
2378	verbatimSymlinks <boolean> When true, path resolution for symlinks will
2379	be skipped. Default: false
2380	
2381	
2382	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
2383	
2384	Asynchronously copies the entire directory structure from src to dest,
2385	including subdirectories and files.
2386	When copying a directory to another directory, globs are not supported and
2387	behavior is similar to cp dir1/ dir2/.

2414	exclude <Function> | <string[]> Function to filter out files/directories or a
2415	list of glob patterns to be excluded. If a function is provided, return
2416	true to exclude the item, false to include it. Default: undefined.
2417	If a string array is provided, each string should be a glob pattern that
2418	specifies paths to exclude. Note: Negation patterns (e.g., '!foo.js') are
2419	not supported.

2421	followed while expanding ** patterns. Default: false.

2435	console.log(entry);const { glob } = require('node:fs/promises');

2438	for await (const entry of glob('**/*.js'))

2529	fsPromises.mkdir(path[, options])#
2530	
2531	Added in: v10.0.0
2532	
2533	
2534	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
2535	options <Object> | <integer>
2536	
2537	recursive <boolean> Default: false
2538	mode <string> | <integer> Not supported on Windows. See File modes
2539	for more details. Default: 0o777.
2540	
2541	
2542	Returns: <Promise> Upon success, fulfills with undefined if recursive
2543	is false, or the first directory path created if recursive is true.
2544	
2545	Asynchronously creates a directory.
2546	The optional options argument can be an integer specifying mode (permission
2547	and sticky bits), or an object with a mode property and a recursive
2548	property indicating whether parent directories should be created. Calling
2549	fsPromises.mkdir() when path is a directory that exists results in a
2550	rejection only when recursive is false.
2551	
2552	import { mkdir } from 'node:fs/promises';
2553	
2554	try {
2555	const projectFolder = new URL('./test/project/', import.meta.url);
2556	const createDir = await mkdir(projectFolder, { recursive: true });
2557	
2558	console.log(`created ${createDir}`);
2559	} catch (err) {
2560	console.error(err.message);
2561	}const { mkdir } = require('node:fs/promises');
2562	const { join } = require('node:path');
2563	
2564	async function makeDirectory() {
2565	const projectFolder = join(__dirname, 'test', 'project');
2566	const dirCreation = await mkdir(projectFolder, { recursive: true });
2567	
2568	console.log(dirCreation);
2569	return dirCreation;
2570	}

2574	fsPromises.mkdtemp(prefix[, options])#
2575	
2576	History
2577	
2578	VersionChanges
2579	v20.6.0, v18.19.0
2580	The prefix parameter now accepts buffers and URL.
2581	v16.5.0, v14.18.0
2582	The prefix parameter now accepts an empty string.
2583	v10.0.0
2584	Added in: v10.0.0
2585	
2586	
2587	
2588	
2589	prefix <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
2590	options <string> | <Object>
2591	
2592	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
2593	
2594	
2595	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with the created directory path.
2596	If encoding is 'buffer', then the resulting directory
2597	path is returned as a <Buffer>. Otherwise, the path is returned as a
2598	<string> using the specified encoding.
2599	
2600	Creates a unique temporary directory. A unique directory name is generated by
2601	appending six random characters to the end of the provided prefix. Due to
2602	platform inconsistencies, avoid trailing X characters in prefix. Some
2603	platforms, notably the BSDs, can return more than six random characters, and
2604	replace trailing X characters in prefix with random characters.
2605	The optional options argument can be a string specifying an encoding, or an
2606	object with an encoding property specifying the character encoding to use.
2607	import { mkdtemp } from 'node:fs/promises';
2608	import { join } from 'node:path';
2609	import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
2610	
2611	try {
2612	await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'foo-'));
2613	} catch (err) {
2614	console.error(err);
2615	} copy
2616	The fsPromises.mkdtemp() method will append the six randomly selected
2617	characters directly to the prefix string. For instance, given a directory
2618	/tmp, if the intention is to create a temporary directory within /tmp, the
2619	prefix must end with a trailing platform-specific path separator
2620	(require('node:path').sep).
2621	
2622	fsPromises.mkdtempDisposable(prefix[, options])#
2623	
2624	Added in: v24.4.0
2625	
2626	
2627	prefix <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
2628	options <string> | <Object>
2629	
2630	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
2631	
2632	
2633	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with a Promise for an async-disposable Object:
2634	
2635	path <string> | <Buffer> The path of the created directory.
2636	remove <AsyncFunction> A function which removes the created directory.
2637	[Symbol.asyncDispose] <AsyncFunction> The same as remove.
2638	
2639	
2640	
2641	The resulting Promise holds an async-disposable object whose path property
2642	holds the created directory path. If encoding is 'buffer', the path will
2643	also be a <Buffer>, otherwise a <string>. When the object is disposed, the
2644	directory and its contents will be removed asynchronously if it still exists. If
2645	the directory cannot be deleted, disposal will throw an error. The object has an
2646	async remove() method which will perform the same task.
2647	Both this function and the disposal function on the resulting object are
2648	async, so it should be used with await + await using as in
2649	await using dir = await fsPromises.mkdtempDisposable('prefix').
2650	See the MDN documentation on using statements for more information about
2651	explicit resource management.
2652	For detailed information, see the documentation of fsPromises.mkdtemp().
2653	The optional options argument can be a string specifying an encoding, or an
2654	object with an encoding property specifying the character encoding to use.
2655

2700	options <Object>
2701	
2702	encoding <string> | <null> Default: 'utf8'
2703	bufferSize <number> Number of directory entries that are buffered
2704	internally when reading from the directory. Higher values lead to better
2705	performance but higher memory usage. Default: 32
2706	recursive <boolean> Resolved Dir will be an <AsyncIterable>
2707	containing all sub files and directories. Default: false

2713	opendir(3) documentation for more detail.
2714	Creates an <fs.Dir>, which contains all further functions for reading from
2715	and cleaning up the directory.

2717	directory and subsequent read operations.

2719	import { opendir } from 'node:fs/promises';

2727	} copy
2728	When using the async iterator, the <fs.Dir> object will be automatically
2729	closed after the iterator exits.

2776	fsPromises.readFile(path[, options])#

2778	History

2780	VersionChanges
2781	v24.19.0
2782	Added support for the buffer option.
2783	v15.2.0, v14.17.0

2787	
2788	
2789	
2790	
2791	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> | <FileHandle> filename or FileHandle
2792	options <Object> | <string>
2793	
2794	encoding <string> | <null> Default: null
2795	flag <string> See support of file system flags. Default: 'r'.
2796	signal <AbortSignal> allows aborting an in-progress readFile
2797	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> | <Function> A buffer to read into, or a
2798	function called with the file size that returns the buffer.
2799	
2800	
2801	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with the contents of the file.
2802	
2803	Asynchronously reads the entire contents of a file.
2804	If no encoding is specified (using options.encoding), the data is returned
2805	as a <Buffer> object. Otherwise, the data will be a string.
2806	If options is a string, then it specifies the encoding.
2807	If buffer is provided and no encoding is specified, the returned <Buffer> is
2808	a view over the supplied buffer containing only the bytes read. If the
2809	supplied buffer is too small to contain the entire file, the promise will be
2810	rejected.
2811	When the path is a directory, the behavior of fsPromises.readFile() is
2812	platform-specific. On macOS, Linux, and Windows, the promise will be rejected
2813	with an error. On FreeBSD, a representation of the directory's contents will be
2814	returned.
2815	An example of reading a package.json file located in the same directory of the
2816	running code:
2817	
2818	import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
2819	try {
2820	const filePath = new URL('./package.json', import.meta.url);
2821	const contents = await readFile(filePath, { encoding: 'utf8' });
2822	console.log(contents);
2823	} catch (err) {
2824	console.error(err.message);
2825	}const { readFile } = require('node:fs/promises');
2826	const { resolve } = require('node:path');
2827	async function logFile() {
2828	try {
2829	const filePath = resolve('./package.json');
2830	const contents = await readFile(filePath, { encoding: 'utf8' });
2831	console.log(contents);
2832	} catch (err) {
2833	console.error(err.message);
2834	}
2835	}
2836	logFile();copy
2837	It is possible to abort an ongoing readFile using an <AbortSignal>. If a
2838	request is aborted the promise returned is rejected with an AbortError:
2839	import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
2840	
2841	try {
2842	const controller = new AbortController();
2843	const { signal } = controller;
2844	const promise = readFile(fileName, { signal });
2845	
2846	// Abort the request before the promise settles.
2847	controller.abort();
2848	
2849	await promise;
2850	} catch (err) {
2851	// When a request is aborted - err is an AbortError
2852	console.error(err);
2853	} copy
2854	Aborting an ongoing request does not abort individual operating
2855	system requests but rather the internal buffering fs.readFile performs.
2856	Any specified <FileHandle> has to support reading.
2857	An example using the buffer option with a pre-allocated buffer:
2858	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
2859	import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
2860	
2861	const buf = Buffer.alloc(16384);
2862	const contents = await readFile('/path/to/file', { buffer: buf });
2863	console.log(contents); // A view over `buf` containing only the bytes read copy
2864	An example using the buffer option with a function returning a buffer:
2865	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
2866	import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
2867	
2868	const contents = await readFile('/path/to/file', {
2869	buffer: (size) => Buffer.alloc(size),
2870	});
2871	console.log(contents); copy

2873	fsPromises.readlink(path[, options])#
2874	
2875	Added in: v10.0.0
2876	
2877	
2878	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
2879	options <string> | <Object>
2880	
2881	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
2882	
2883	
2884	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with the linkString upon success.
2885	
2886	Reads the contents of the symbolic link referred to by path. See the POSIX
2887	readlink(2) documentation for more detail. The promise is fulfilled with the
2888	linkString upon success.
2889	The optional options argument can be a string specifying an encoding, or an
2890	object with an encoding property specifying the character encoding to use for
2891	the link path returned. If the encoding is set to 'buffer', the link path
2892	returned will be passed as a <Buffer> object.

2894	fsPromises.realpath(path[, options])#
2895	
2896	Added in: v10.0.0
2897	
2898	
2899	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
2900	options <string> | <Object>
2901	
2902	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
2903	
2904	
2905	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with the resolved path upon success.
2906	
2907	Determines the actual location of path using the same semantics as the
2908	fs.realpath.native() function.
2909	Only paths that can be converted to UTF8 strings are supported.
2910	The optional options argument can be a string specifying an encoding, or an
2911	object with an encoding property specifying the character encoding to use for
2912	the path. If the encoding is set to 'buffer', the path returned will be
2913	passed as a <Buffer> object.
2914	On Linux, when Node.js is linked against musl libc, the procfs file system must
2915	be mounted on /proc in order for this function to work. Glibc does not have
2916	this restriction.

2929	fsPromises.rmdir(path[, options])#
2930	
2931	History
2932	
2933	VersionChanges
2934	v16.0.0
2935	Using fsPromises.rmdir(path, { recursive: true }) on a path that is a file is no longer permitted and results in an ENOENT error on Windows and an ENOTDIR error on POSIX.
2936	v16.0.0
2937	Using fsPromises.rmdir(path, { recursive: true }) on a path that does not exist is no longer permitted and results in a ENOENT error.
2938	v16.0.0
2939	The recursive option is deprecated, using it triggers a deprecation warning.
2940	v14.14.0
2941	The recursive option is deprecated, use fsPromises.rm instead.
2942	v13.3.0, v12.16.0
2943	The maxBusyTries option is renamed to maxRetries, and its default is 0. The emfileWait option has been removed, and EMFILE errors use the same retry logic as other errors. The retryDelay option is now supported. ENFILE errors are now retried.
2944	v12.10.0
2945	The recursive, maxBusyTries, and emfileWait options are now supported.
2946	v10.0.0
2947	Added in: v10.0.0
2948	
2949	
2950	
2951	
2952	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
2953	options <Object>
2954	
2955	maxRetries <integer> If an EBUSY, EMFILE, ENFILE, ENOTEMPTY, or
2956	EPERM error is encountered, Node.js retries the operation with a linear
2957	backoff wait of retryDelay milliseconds longer on each try. This option
2958	represents the number of retries. This option is ignored if the recursive
2959	option is not true. Default: 0.
2960	recursive <boolean> If true, perform a recursive directory removal. In
2961	recursive mode, operations are retried on failure. Default: false.
2962	Deprecated.
2963	retryDelay <integer> The amount of time in milliseconds to wait between
2964	retries. This option is ignored if the recursive option is not true.
2965	Default: 100.
2966	
2967	
2968	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
2969	
2970	Removes the directory identified by path.
2971	Using fsPromises.rmdir() on a file (not a directory) results in the
2972	promise being rejected with an ENOENT error on Windows and an ENOTDIR
2973	error on POSIX.
2974	To get a behavior similar to the rm -rf Unix command, use
2975	fsPromises.rm() with options { recursive: true, force: true }.
2976

2990	represents the number of retries. This option is ignored if the recursive

3048	fsPromises.symlink(target, path[, type])#
3049	
3050	History
3051	
3052	VersionChanges
3053	v19.0.0
3054	If the type argument is null or omitted, Node.js will autodetect target type and automatically select dir or file.
3055	v10.0.0

3057

3064	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
3065	
3066	Creates a symbolic link.
3067	The type argument is only used on Windows platforms and can be one of 'dir',
3068	'file', or 'junction'. If the type argument is null, Node.js will
3069	autodetect target type and use 'file' or 'dir'. If the target does not
3070	exist, 'file' will be used. Windows junction points require the destination
3071	path to be absolute. When using 'junction', the target argument will
3072	automatically be normalized to absolute path. Junction points on NTFS volumes
3073	can only point to directories.

3119	fsPromises.watch(filename[, options])#
3120	
3121	Added in: v15.9.0, v14.18.0
3122	
3123	
3124	filename <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
3125	options <string> | <Object>
3126	
3127	persistent <boolean> Indicates whether the process should continue to run
3128	as long as files are being watched. Default: true.
3129	recursive <boolean> Indicates whether all subdirectories should be
3130	watched, or only the current directory. This applies when a directory is
3131	specified, and only on supported platforms (See caveats). Default:
3132	false.
3133	encoding <string> Specifies the character encoding to be used for the
3134	filename passed to the listener. Default: 'utf8'.
3135	signal <AbortSignal> An <AbortSignal> used to signal when the watcher
3136	should stop.
3137	maxQueue <number> Specifies the number of events to queue between iterations
3138	of the <AsyncIterator> returned. Default: 2048.
3139	overflow <string> Either 'ignore' or 'error' when there are more events to be
3140	queued than maxQueue allows. 'ignore' means overflow events are dropped and a
3141	warning is emitted, while 'error' means to throw an exception. Default: 'ignore'.
3142	ignore <string> | <RegExp> | <Function> | <Array> Pattern(s) to ignore. Strings are
3143	glob patterns (using minimatch), RegExp patterns are tested against
3144	the filename, and functions receive the filename and return true to
3145	ignore. Default: undefined.
3146	
3147	
3148	Returns: <AsyncIterator> of objects with the properties:
3149	
3150	eventType <string> The type of change
3151	filename <string> | <Buffer> | <null> The name of the file changed.
3152	
3153	
3154	
3155	Returns an async iterator that watches for changes on filename, where filename
3156	is either a file or a directory.
3157	const { watch } = require('node:fs/promises');
3158	
3159	const ac = new AbortController();
3160	const { signal } = ac;
3161	setTimeout(() => ac.abort(), 10000);
3162	
3163	(async () => {
3164	try {
3165	const watcher = watch(__filename, { signal });
3166	for await (const event of watcher)
3167	console.log(event);
3168	} catch (err) {
3169	if (err.name === 'AbortError')
3170	return;
3171	throw err;
3172	}
3173	})(); copy
3174	On most platforms, 'rename' is emitted whenever a filename appears or
3175	disappears in the directory.
3176	All the caveats for fs.watch() also apply to fsPromises.watch().

3178	fsPromises.writeFile(file, data[, options])#
3179	
3180	History
3181	
3182	VersionChanges
3183	v21.0.0, v20.10.0
3184	The flush option is now supported.
3185	v15.14.0, v14.18.0
3186	The data argument supports AsyncIterable, Iterable, and Stream.
3187	v15.2.0, v14.17.0
3188	The options argument may include an AbortSignal to abort an ongoing writeFile request.
3189	v14.0.0
3190	The data parameter won't coerce unsupported input to strings anymore.
3191	v10.0.0
3192	Added in: v10.0.0
3193	
3194	
3195	
3196	
3197	file <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> | <FileHandle> filename or FileHandle
3198	data <string> | <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> | <AsyncIterable> | <Iterable>
3199	options <Object> | <string>
3200	
3201	encoding <string> | <null> Default: 'utf8'
3202	mode <integer> Default: 0o666
3203	flag <string> See support of file system flags. Default: 'w'.
3204	flush <boolean> If all data is successfully written to the file, and
3205	flush is true, filehandle.sync() is used to flush the data.
3206	Default: false.
3207	signal <AbortSignal> allows aborting an in-progress writeFile
3208	
3209	
3210	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with undefined upon success.
3211	
3212	Asynchronously writes data to a file, replacing the file if it already exists.
3213	data can be a string, a buffer, an <AsyncIterable>, or an <Iterable> object.
3214	The encoding option is ignored if data is a buffer.
3215	If options is a string, then it specifies the encoding.
3216	The mode option only affects the newly created file. See fs.open()
3217	for more details.
3218	Any specified <FileHandle> has to support writing.
3219	It is unsafe to use fsPromises.writeFile() multiple times on the same file
3220	without waiting for the promise to be settled.
3221	Similarly to fsPromises.readFile - fsPromises.writeFile is a convenience
3222	method that performs multiple write calls internally to write the buffer
3223	passed to it. For performance sensitive code consider using
3224	fs.createWriteStream() or filehandle.createWriteStream().
3225	It is possible to use an <AbortSignal> to cancel an fsPromises.writeFile().
3226	Cancelation is "best effort", and some amount of data is likely still
3227	to be written.
3228	import { writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
3229	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
3230	
3231	try {
3232	const controller = new AbortController();
3233	const { signal } = controller;
3234	const data = new Uint8Array(Buffer.from('Hello Node.js'));
3235	const promise = writeFile('message.txt', data, { signal });
3236	
3237	// Abort the request before the promise settles.
3238	controller.abort();
3239	
3240	await promise;
3241	} catch (err) {
3242	// When a request is aborted - err is an AbortError
3243	console.error(err);
3244	} copy
3245	Aborting an ongoing request does not abort individual operating
3246	system requests but rather the internal buffering fs.writeFile performs.

3248	fsPromises.constants#
3249	
3250	Added in: v18.4.0, v16.17.0
3251	
3252	
3253	Type: <Object>
3254	
3255	Returns an object containing commonly used constants for file system
3256	operations. The object is the same as fs.constants. See FS constants
3257	for more details.
3258	
3259	Callback API#
3260	The callback APIs perform all operations asynchronously, without blocking the
3261	event loop, then invoke a callback function upon completion or error.
3262	The callback APIs use the underlying Node.js threadpool to perform file
3263	system operations off the event loop thread. These operations are not
3264	synchronized or threadsafe. Care must be taken when performing multiple
3265	concurrent modifications on the same file or data corruption may occur.
3266	
3267	fs.access(path[, mode], callback)#
3268	
3269	History
3270	
3271	VersionChanges
3272	v20.8.0
3273	The constants fs.F_OK, fs.R_OK, fs.W_OK and fs.X_OK which were present directly on fs are deprecated.
3274	v18.0.0
3275	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
3276	v7.6.0
3277	The path parameter can be a WHATWG URL object using file: protocol.
3278	v6.3.0
3279	The constants like fs.R_OK, etc which were present directly on fs were moved into fs.constants as a soft deprecation. Thus for Node.js < v6.3.0 use fs to access those constants, or do something like (fs.constants || fs).R_OK to work with all versions.
3280	v0.11.15
3281	Added in: v0.11.15
3282	
3283	
3284	
3285	
3286	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
3287	mode <integer> Default: fs.constants.F_OK
3288	callback <Function>
3289	
3290	err <Error>
3291	
3292	
3293	
3294	Tests a user's permissions for the file or directory specified by path.
3295	The mode argument is an optional integer that specifies the accessibility
3296	checks to be performed. mode should be either the value fs.constants.F_OK
3297	or a mask consisting of the bitwise OR of any of fs.constants.R_OK,
3298	fs.constants.W_OK, and fs.constants.X_OK (e.g.
3299	fs.constants.W_OK | fs.constants.R_OK). Check File access constants for
3300	possible values of mode.
3301	The final argument, callback, is a callback function that is invoked with
3302	a possible error argument. If any of the accessibility checks fail, the error
3303	argument will be an Error object. The following examples check if
3304	package.json exists, and if it is readable or writable.
3305	import { access, constants } from 'node:fs';
3306	
3307	const file = 'package.json';
3308	
3309	// Check if the file exists in the current directory.
3310	access(file, constants.F_OK, (err) => {
3311	console.log(`${file} ${err ? 'does not exist' : 'exists'}`);
3312	});
3313	
3314	// Check if the file is readable.
3315	access(file, constants.R_OK, (err) => {
3316	console.log(`${file} ${err ? 'is not readable' : 'is readable'}`);
3317	});
3318	
3319	// Check if the file is writable.
3320	access(file, constants.W_OK, (err) => {
3321	console.log(`${file} ${err ? 'is not writable' : 'is writable'}`);
3322	});
3323	
3324	// Check if the file is readable and writable.
3325	access(file, constants.R_OK | constants.W_OK, (err) => {
3326	console.log(`${file} ${err ? 'is not' : 'is'} readable and writable`);
3327	}); copy
3328	Do not use fs.access() to check for the accessibility of a file before calling
3329	fs.open(), fs.readFile(), or fs.writeFile(). Doing
3330	so introduces a race condition, since other processes may change the file's
3331	state between the two calls. Instead, user code should open/read/write the
3332	file directly and handle the error raised if the file is not accessible.
3333	write (NOT RECOMMENDED)
3334	import { access, open, close } from 'node:fs';
3335	
3336	access('myfile', (err) => {
3337	if (!err) {
3338	console.error('myfile already exists');
3339	return;
3340	}
3341	
3342	open('myfile', 'wx', (err, fd) => {
3343	if (err) throw err;
3344	
3345	try {
3346	writeMyData(fd);
3347	} finally {
3348	close(fd, (err) => {
3349	if (err) throw err;
3350	});
3351	}
3352	});
3353	}); copy
3354	write (RECOMMENDED)
3355	import { open, close } from 'node:fs';
3356	
3357	open('myfile', 'wx', (err, fd) => {
3358	if (err) {
3359	if (err.code === 'EEXIST') {
3360	console.error('myfile already exists');
3361	return;
3362	}
3363	
3364	throw err;
3365	}
3366	
3367	try {
3368	writeMyData(fd);
3369	} finally {
3370	close(fd, (err) => {
3371	if (err) throw err;
3372	});
3373	}
3374	}); copy
3375	read (NOT RECOMMENDED)
3376	import { access, open, close } from 'node:fs';
3377	access('myfile', (err) => {
3378	if (err) {
3379	if (err.code === 'ENOENT') {
3380	console.error('myfile does not exist');
3381	return;
3382	}
3383	
3384	throw err;
3385	}
3386	
3387	open('myfile', 'r', (err, fd) => {
3388	if (err) throw err;
3389	
3390	try {
3391	readMyData(fd);
3392	} finally {
3393	close(fd, (err) => {
3394	if (err) throw err;
3395	});
3396	}
3397	});
3398	}); copy
3399	read (RECOMMENDED)
3400	import { open, close } from 'node:fs';
3401	
3402	open('myfile', 'r', (err, fd) => {
3403	if (err) {
3404	if (err.code === 'ENOENT') {
3405	console.error('myfile does not exist');
3406	return;
3407	}
3408	
3409	throw err;
3410	}
3411	
3412	try {
3413	readMyData(fd);
3414	} finally {
3415	close(fd, (err) => {
3416	if (err) throw err;
3417	});
3418	}
3419	}); copy
3420	The "not recommended" examples above check for accessibility and then use the
3421	file; the "recommended" examples are better because they use the file directly
3422	and handle the error, if any.
3423	In general, check for the accessibility of a file only if the file will not be
3424	used directly, for example when its accessibility is a signal from another
3425	process.
3426	On Windows, access-control policies (ACLs) on a directory may limit access to
3427	a file or directory. The fs.access() function, however, does not check the
3428	ACL and therefore may report that a path is accessible even if the ACL restricts
3429	the user from reading or writing to it.
3430	
3431	fs.appendFile(path, data[, options], callback)#
3432	
3433	History
3434	
3435	VersionChanges
3436	v21.1.0, v20.10.0
3437	The flush option is now supported.
3438	v18.0.0
3439	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
3440	v10.0.0
3441	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will throw a TypeError at runtime.
3442	v7.0.0
3443	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will emit a deprecation warning with id DEP0013.
3444	v7.0.0
3445	The passed options object will never be modified.
3446	v5.0.0
3447	The file parameter can be a file descriptor now.
3448	v0.6.7
3449	Added in: v0.6.7
3450	
3451	
3452	
3453	
3454	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> | <number> filename or file descriptor
3455	data <string> | <Buffer>
3456	options <Object> | <string>
3457	
3458	encoding <string> | <null> Default: 'utf8'
3459	mode <integer> Default: 0o666
3460	flag <string> See support of file system flags. Default: 'a'.
3461	flush <boolean> If true, the underlying file descriptor is flushed
3462	prior to closing it. Default: false.
3463	
3464	
3465	callback <Function>
3466	
3467	err <Error>
3468	
3469	
3470	
3471	Asynchronously append data to a file, creating the file if it does not yet
3472	exist. data can be a string or a <Buffer>.
3473	The mode option only affects the newly created file. See fs.open()
3474	for more details.
3475	import { appendFile } from 'node:fs';
3476	
3477	appendFile('message.txt', 'data to append', (err) => {
3478	if (err) throw err;
3479	console.log('The "data to append" was appended to file!');
3480	}); copy
3481	If options is a string, then it specifies the encoding:
3482	import { appendFile } from 'node:fs';
3483	
3484	appendFile('message.txt', 'data to append', 'utf8', callback); copy
3485	The path may be specified as a numeric file descriptor that has been opened
3486	for appending (using fs.open() or fs.openSync()). The file descriptor will
3487	not be closed automatically.
3488	import { open, close, appendFile } from 'node:fs';
3489	
3490	function closeFd(fd) {
3491	close(fd, (err) => {
3492	if (err) throw err;
3493	});
3494	}
3495	
3496	open('message.txt', 'a', (err, fd) => {
3497	if (err) throw err;
3498	
3499	try {
3500	appendFile(fd, 'data to append', 'utf8', (err) => {
3501	closeFd(fd);
3502	if (err) throw err;
3503	});
3504	} catch (err) {
3505	closeFd(fd);
3506	throw err;
3507	}
3508	}); copy

3546	
3547	File modes#
3548	The mode argument used in both the fs.chmod() and fs.chmodSync()
3549	methods is a numeric bitmask created using a logical OR of the following
3550	constants:
3551	
3552	
3553	
3554	
3555	
3556

3600	
3601	
3602	
3603	
3604	
3605	
3606	ConstantOctalDescriptionfs.constants.S_IRUSR0o400read by ownerfs.constants.S_IWUSR0o200write by ownerfs.constants.S_IXUSR0o100execute/search by ownerfs.constants.S_IRGRP0o40read by groupfs.constants.S_IWGRP0o20write by groupfs.constants.S_IXGRP0o10execute/search by groupfs.constants.S_IROTH0o4read by othersfs.constants.S_IWOTH0o2write by othersfs.constants.S_IXOTH0o1execute/search by others
3607	An easier method of constructing the mode is to use a sequence of three
3608	octal digits (e.g. 765). The left-most digit (7 in the example), specifies
3609	the permissions for the file owner. The middle digit (6 in the example),
3610	specifies permissions for the group. The right-most digit (5 in the example),
3611	specifies the permissions for others.
3612

3614

3616

3641

3647	
3648	
3649	
3650	
3651	
3652	
3653	NumberDescription7read, write, and execute6read and write5read and execute4read only3write and execute2write only1execute only0no permission
3654	For example, the octal value 0o765 means:
3655	
3656	The owner may read, write, and execute the file.
3657	The group may read and write the file.
3658	Others may read and execute the file.
3659	
3660	When using raw numbers where file modes are expected, any value larger than
3661	0o777 may result in platform-specific behaviors that are not supported to work
3662	consistently. Therefore constants like S_ISVTX, S_ISGID, or S_ISUID are
3663	not exposed in fs.constants.
3664	Caveats: on Windows only the write permission can be changed, and the
3665	distinction among the permissions of group, owner, or others is not
3666	implemented.

3732	fs.copyFile(src, dest[, mode], callback)#
3733	
3734	History
3735	
3736	VersionChanges
3737	v18.0.0
3738	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
3739	v14.0.0
3740	Changed flags argument to mode and imposed stricter type validation.
3741	v8.5.0
3742	Added in: v8.5.0
3743	
3744	
3745	
3746	
3747	src <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> source filename to copy
3748	dest <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> destination filename of the copy operation
3749	mode <integer> modifiers for copy operation. Default: 0.
3750	callback <Function>
3751	
3752	err <Error>
3753	
3754	
3755	
3756	Asynchronously copies src to dest. By default, dest is overwritten if it
3757	already exists. No arguments other than a possible exception are given to the
3758	callback function. Node.js makes no guarantees about the atomicity of the copy
3759	operation. If an error occurs after the destination file has been opened for
3760	writing, Node.js will attempt to remove the destination.
3761	Symbolic links are followed. If src is a symbolic link, the target file is
3762	copied. If dest is a symbolic link, the target file is overwritten unless
3763	mode contains fs.constants.COPYFILE_EXCL.
3764	mode is an optional integer that specifies the behavior
3765	of the copy operation. It is possible to create a mask consisting of the bitwise
3766	OR of two or more values (e.g.
3767	fs.constants.COPYFILE_EXCL | fs.constants.COPYFILE_FICLONE).
3768	
3769	fs.constants.COPYFILE_EXCL: The copy operation will fail if dest already
3770	exists.
3771	fs.constants.COPYFILE_FICLONE: The copy operation will attempt to create a
3772	copy-on-write reflink. If the platform does not support copy-on-write, then a
3773	fallback copy mechanism is used.
3774	fs.constants.COPYFILE_FICLONE_FORCE: The copy operation will attempt to
3775	create a copy-on-write reflink. If the platform does not support
3776	copy-on-write, then the operation will fail.
3777	
3778	import { copyFile, constants } from 'node:fs';
3779	
3780	function callback(err) {
3781	if (err) throw err;
3782	console.log('source.txt was copied to destination.txt');
3783	}
3784	
3785	// destination.txt will be created or overwritten by default.
3786	copyFile('source.txt', 'destination.txt', callback);
3787	
3788	// By using COPYFILE_EXCL, the operation will fail if destination.txt exists.
3789	copyFile('source.txt', 'destination.txt', constants.COPYFILE_EXCL, callback); copy
3790

3802	v17.6.0, v16.15.0

3808	
3809	
3810	src <string> | <URL> source path to copy.
3811	dest <string> | <URL> destination path to copy to.
3812	options <Object>
3813	
3814	dereference <boolean> dereference symlinks. Default: false.
3815	errorOnExist <boolean> when force is false, and the destination
3816	exists, throw an error. Default: false.
3817	filter <Function> Function to filter copied files/directories. Return
3818	true to copy the item, false to ignore it. When ignoring a directory,
3819	all of its contents will be skipped as well. Can also return a Promise
3820	that fulfills with true or false. Default: undefined.
3821	
3822	src <string> source path to copy.
3823	dest <string> destination path to copy to.
3824	Returns: <boolean> | <Promise> A value that is coercible to boolean or
3825	a Promise that fulfils with such value.
3826	
3827	
3828	force <boolean> overwrite existing file or directory. The copy
3829	operation will ignore errors if you set this to false and the destination
3830	exists. Use the errorOnExist option to change this behavior.
3831	Default: true.
3832	mode <integer> modifiers for copy operation. Default: 0.
3833	See mode flag of fs.copyFile().

3835	be preserved. Default: false.
3836	recursive <boolean> copy directories recursively Default: false

3838	be skipped. Default: false
3839

3841	callback <Function>
3842	
3843	err <Error>
3844	
3845	
3846	
3847	Asynchronously copies the entire directory structure from src to dest,
3848	including subdirectories and files.
3849	When copying a directory to another directory, globs are not supported and
3850	behavior is similar to cp dir1/ dir2/.

3852	fs.createReadStream(path[, options])#
3853	
3854	History
3855	
3856	VersionChanges
3857	v24.21.0
3858	Add the windowsHandle option.
3859	v16.10.0
3860	The fs option does not need open method if an fd was provided.
3861	v16.10.0
3862	The fs option does not need close method if autoClose is false.
3863	v15.5.0
3864	Add support for AbortSignal.
3865	v15.4.0
3866	The fd option accepts FileHandle arguments.
3867	v14.0.0
3868	Change emitClose default to true.
3869	v13.6.0, v12.17.0
3870	The fs options allow overriding the used fs implementation.
3871	v12.10.0
3872	Enable emitClose option.
3873	v11.0.0
3874	Impose new restrictions on start and end, throwing more appropriate errors in cases when we cannot reasonably handle the input values.
3875	v7.6.0
3876	The path parameter can be a WHATWG URL object using file: protocol.
3877	v7.0.0
3878	The passed options object will never be modified.
3879	v2.3.0
3880	The passed options object can be a string now.
3881	v0.1.31
3882	Added in: v0.1.31
3883	
3884	
3885	
3886	
3887	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
3888	options <string> | <Object>
3889	
3890	flags <string> See support of file system flags. Default:
3891	'r'.
3892	encoding <string> Default: null
3893	fd <integer> | <FileHandle> Default: null
3894	mode <integer> Default: 0o666
3895	autoClose <boolean> Default: true
3896	emitClose <boolean> Default: true
3897	start <integer>
3898	end <integer> Default: Infinity
3899	highWaterMark <integer> Default: 64 * 1024
3900	fs <Object> | <null> Default: null
3901	signal <AbortSignal> | <null> Default: null
3902	windowsHandle <bigint> A raw Win32 HANDLE value to read from, in place
3903	of fd. Windows only. Default: null
3904	
3905	
3906	Returns: <fs.ReadStream>
3907	
3908	options can include start and end values to read a range of bytes from
3909	the file instead of the entire file. Both start and end are inclusive and
3910	start counting at 0, allowed values are in the
3911	[0, Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER] range. If fd is specified and start is
3912	omitted or undefined, fs.createReadStream() reads sequentially from the
3913	current file position. The encoding can be any one of those accepted by
3914	<Buffer>.
3915	If fd is specified, ReadStream will ignore the path argument and will use
3916	the specified file descriptor. This means that no 'open' event will be
3917	emitted. fd should be blocking; non-blocking fd s should be passed to
3918	<net.Socket>.
3919	If fd points to a character device that only supports blocking reads
3920	(such as keyboard or sound card), read operations do not finish until data is
3921	available. This can prevent the process from exiting and the stream from
3922	closing naturally.
3923	On Windows, a value passed in fd is interpreted as a CRT file descriptor. To
3924	use a raw Win32 HANDLE instead, such as an inherited anonymous pipe handle
3925	obtained from another process, pass it as windowsHandle. The handle is wrapped
3926	in a file descriptor that the stream owns and closes. The windowsHandle option
3927	throws on non-Windows platforms and cannot be combined with the fs option.
3928	By default, the stream will emit a 'close' event after it has been
3929	destroyed. Set the emitClose option to false to change this behavior.
3930	By providing the fs option, it is possible to override the corresponding fs
3931	implementations for open, read, and close. When providing the fs option,
3932	an override for read is required. If no fd is provided, an override for
3933	open is also required. If autoClose is true, an override for close is
3934	also required.
3935	import { createReadStream } from 'node:fs';
3936	
3937	// Create a stream from some character device.
3938	const stream = createReadStream('/dev/input/event0');
3939	setTimeout(() => {
3940	stream.close(); // This may not close the stream.
3941	// Artificially marking end-of-stream, as if the underlying resource had
3942	// indicated end-of-file by itself, allows the stream to close.
3943	// This does not cancel pending read operations, and if there is such an
3944	// operation, the process may still not be able to exit successfully
3945	// until it finishes.
3946	stream.push(null);
3947	stream.read(0);
3948	}, 100); copy
3949	If autoClose is false, then the file descriptor won't be closed, even if
3950	there's an error. It is the application's responsibility to close it and make
3951	sure there's no file descriptor leak. If autoClose is set to true (default
3952	behavior), on 'error' or 'end' the file descriptor will be closed
3953	automatically.
3954	mode sets the file mode (permission and sticky bits), but only if the
3955	file was created.
3956	An example to read the last 10 bytes of a file which is 100 bytes long:
3957	import { createReadStream } from 'node:fs';
3958	
3959	createReadStream('sample.txt', { start: 90, end: 99 }); copy
3960	If options is a string, then it specifies the encoding.
3961

4000	
4001	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
4002	options <string> | <Object>
4003	
4004	flags <string> See support of file system flags. Default:
4005	'w'.
4006	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
4007	fd <integer> | <FileHandle> Default: null
4008	mode <integer> Default: 0o666
4009	autoClose <boolean> Default: true
4010	emitClose <boolean> Default: true
4011	start <integer>
4012	fs <Object> | <null> Default: null
4013	signal <AbortSignal> | <null> Default: null
4014	highWaterMark <number> Default: See
4015	stream.getDefaultHighWaterMark().
4016	flush <boolean> If true, the underlying file descriptor is flushed
4017	prior to closing it. Default: false.
4018	windowsHandle <bigint> A raw Win32 HANDLE value to write to, in place
4019	of fd. Windows only. Default: null
4020	
4021	
4022	Returns: <fs.WriteStream>
4023	
4024	options may also include a start option to allow writing data at some
4025	position past the beginning of the file, allowed values are in the
4026	[0, Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER] range. Modifying a file rather than
4027	replacing it may require the flags option to be set to r+ rather than the
4028	default w. The encoding can be any one of those accepted by <Buffer>.
4029	If autoClose is set to true (default behavior) on 'error' or 'finish'
4030	the file descriptor will be closed automatically. If autoClose is false,
4031	then the file descriptor won't be closed, even if there's an error.
4032	It is the application's responsibility to close it and make sure there's no
4033	file descriptor leak.
4034	On Windows, a value passed in fd is interpreted as a CRT file descriptor. To
4035	use a raw Win32 HANDLE instead, such as an inherited anonymous pipe handle
4036	obtained from another process, pass it as windowsHandle. The handle is wrapped
4037	in a file descriptor that the stream owns and closes. The windowsHandle option
4038	throws on non-Windows platforms and cannot be combined with the fs option.
4039	By default, the stream will emit a 'close' event after it has been
4040	destroyed. Set the emitClose option to false to change this behavior.
4041	By providing the fs option it is possible to override the corresponding fs
4042	implementations for open, write, writev, and close. Overriding write()
4043	without writev() can reduce performance as some optimizations (_writev())
4044	will be disabled. When providing the fs option, overrides for at least one of
4045	write and writev are required. If no fd option is supplied, an override
4046	for open is also required. If autoClose is true, an override for close
4047	is also required.
4048	Like <fs.ReadStream>, if fd is specified, <fs.WriteStream> will ignore the
4049	path argument and will use the specified file descriptor. This means that no
4050	'open' event will be emitted. fd should be blocking; non-blocking fd s
4051	should be passed to <net.Socket>.
4052	If options is a string, then it specifies the encoding.
