4070	Stability: 0 - Deprecated: Use fs.stat() or fs.access() instead.
4071	
4072	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
4073	callback <Function>
4074	
4075	exists <boolean>
4076	
4077	
4078	
4079	Test whether or not the element at the given path exists by checking with the file system.
4080	Then call the callback argument with either true or false:
4081	import { exists } from 'node:fs';
4082	
4083	exists('/etc/passwd', (e) => {
4084	console.log(e ? 'it exists' : 'no passwd!');
4085	}); copy
4086	The parameters for this callback are not consistent with other Node.js
4087	callbacks. Normally, the first parameter to a Node.js callback is an err
4088	parameter, optionally followed by other parameters. The fs.exists() callback
4089	has only one boolean parameter. This is one reason fs.access() is recommended
4090	instead of fs.exists().
4091	If path is a symbolic link, it is followed. Thus, if path exists but points
4092	to a non-existent element, the callback will receive the value false.
4093	Using fs.exists() to check for the existence of a file before calling
4094	fs.open(), fs.readFile(), or fs.writeFile() is not recommended. Doing
4095	so introduces a race condition, since other processes may change the file's
4096	state between the two calls. Instead, user code should open/read/write the
4097	file directly and handle the error raised if the file does not exist.
4098	write (NOT RECOMMENDED)
4099	import { exists, open, close } from 'node:fs';
4100	
4101	exists('myfile', (e) => {
4102	if (e) {
4103	console.error('myfile already exists');
4104	} else {
4105	open('myfile', 'wx', (err, fd) => {
4106	if (err) throw err;
4107	
4108	try {
4109	writeMyData(fd);
4110	} finally {
4111	close(fd, (err) => {
4112	if (err) throw err;
4113	});
4114	}
4115	});
4116	}
4117	}); copy
4118	write (RECOMMENDED)
4119	import { open, close } from 'node:fs';
4120	open('myfile', 'wx', (err, fd) => {
4121	if (err) {
4122	if (err.code === 'EEXIST') {
4123	console.error('myfile already exists');
4124	return;
4125	}
4126	
4127	throw err;
4128	}
4129	
4130	try {
4131	writeMyData(fd);
4132	} finally {
4133	close(fd, (err) => {
4134	if (err) throw err;
4135	});
4136	}
4137	}); copy
4138	read (NOT RECOMMENDED)
4139	import { open, close, exists } from 'node:fs';
4140	
4141	exists('myfile', (e) => {
4142	if (e) {
4143	open('myfile', 'r', (err, fd) => {
4144	if (err) throw err;
4145	
4146	try {
4147	readMyData(fd);
4148	} finally {
4149	close(fd, (err) => {
4150	if (err) throw err;
4151	});
4152	}
4153	});
4154	} else {
4155	console.error('myfile does not exist');
4156	}
4157	}); copy
4158	read (RECOMMENDED)
4159	import { open, close } from 'node:fs';
4160	
4161	open('myfile', 'r', (err, fd) => {
4162	if (err) {
4163	if (err.code === 'ENOENT') {
4164	console.error('myfile does not exist');
4165	return;
4166	}
4167	
4168	throw err;
4169	}
4170	
4171	try {
4172	readMyData(fd);
4173	} finally {
4174	close(fd, (err) => {
4175	if (err) throw err;
4176	});
4177	}
4178	}); copy
4179	The "not recommended" examples above check for existence and then use the
4180	file; the "recommended" examples are better because they use the file directly
4181	and handle the error, if any.
4182	In general, check for the existence of a file only if the file won't be
4183	used directly, for example when its existence is a signal from another
4184	process.
4185

4339	fs.ftruncate(fd[, len], callback)#
4340	
4341	History
4342	
4343	VersionChanges
4344	v18.0.0
4345	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
4346	v10.0.0
4347	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will throw a TypeError at runtime.
4348	v7.0.0
4349	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will emit a deprecation warning with id DEP0013.
4350	v0.8.6
4351	Added in: v0.8.6
4352	
4353	
4354	
4355	
4356	fd <integer>
4357	len <integer> Default: 0
4358	callback <Function>
4359	
4360	err <Error>
4361	
4362	
4363	
4364	Truncates the file descriptor. No arguments other than a possible exception are
4365	given to the completion callback.
4366	See the POSIX ftruncate(2) documentation for more detail.
4367	If the file referred to by the file descriptor was larger than len bytes, only
4368	the first len bytes will be retained in the file.
4369	For example, the following program retains only the first four bytes of the
4370	file:
4371	import { open, close, ftruncate } from 'node:fs';
4372	
4373	function closeFd(fd) {
4374	close(fd, (err) => {
4375	if (err) throw err;
4376	});
4377	}
4378	
4379	open('temp.txt', 'r+', (err, fd) => {
4380	if (err) throw err;
4381	
4382	try {
4383	ftruncate(fd, 4, (err) => {
4384	closeFd(fd);
4385	if (err) throw err;
4386	});
4387	} catch (err) {
4388	closeFd(fd);
4389	if (err) throw err;
4390	}
4391	}); copy
4392	If the file previously was shorter than len bytes, it is extended, and the
4393	extended part is filled with null bytes ('\0'):
4394	If len is negative then 0 will be used.
4395

4657	fs.mkdir(path[, options], callback)#
4658	
4659	History
4660	
4661	VersionChanges
4662	v18.0.0
4663	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
4664	v13.11.0, v12.17.0
4665	In recursive mode, the callback now receives the first created path as an argument.
4666	v10.12.0
4667	The second argument can now be an options object with recursive and mode properties.
4668	v10.0.0
4669	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will throw a TypeError at runtime.
4670	v7.6.0
4671	The path parameter can be a WHATWG URL object using file: protocol.
4672	v7.0.0
4673	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will emit a deprecation warning with id DEP0013.
4674	v0.1.8
4675	Added in: v0.1.8
4676	
4677	
4678	
4679	
4680	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
4681	options <Object> | <integer>
4682	
4683	recursive <boolean> Default: false
4684	mode <string> | <integer> Not supported on Windows. See File modes
4685	for more details. Default: 0o777.
4686	
4687	
4688	callback <Function>
4689	
4690	err <Error>
4691	path <string> | <undefined> Present only if a directory is created with
4692	recursive set to true.
4693	
4694	
4695	
4696	Asynchronously creates a directory.
4697	The callback is given a possible exception and, if recursive is true, the
4698	first directory path created, (err[, path]).
4699	path can still be undefined when recursive is true, if no directory was
4700	created (for instance, if it was previously created).
4701	The optional options argument can be an integer specifying mode (permission
4702	and sticky bits), or an object with a mode property and a recursive
4703	property indicating whether parent directories should be created. Calling
4704	fs.mkdir() when path is a directory that exists results in an error only
4705	when recursive is false. If recursive is false and the directory exists,
4706	an EEXIST error occurs.
4707	import { mkdir } from 'node:fs';
4708	
4709	// Create ./tmp/a/apple, regardless of whether ./tmp and ./tmp/a exist.
4710	mkdir('./tmp/a/apple', { recursive: true }, (err) => {
4711	if (err) throw err;
4712	}); copy
4713	On Windows, using fs.mkdir() on the root directory even with recursion will
4714	result in an error:
4715	import { mkdir } from 'node:fs';
4716	
4717	mkdir('/', { recursive: true }, (err) => {
4718	// => [Error: EPERM: operation not permitted, mkdir 'C:\']
4719	}); copy
4720	See the POSIX mkdir(2) documentation for more details.

4737	v6.2.1
4738	The callback parameter is optional now.
4739	v5.10.0
4740	Added in: v5.10.0
4741	
4742	
4743	
4744	
4745	prefix <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
4746	options <string> | <Object>
4747	
4748	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
4749	
4750	
4751	callback <Function>
4752	
4753	err <Error>
4754	directory <string> | <Buffer>
4755	
4756	
4757	
4758	Creates a unique temporary directory.
4759	Generates six random characters to be appended behind a required
4760	prefix to create a unique temporary directory. Due to platform
4761	inconsistencies, avoid trailing X characters in prefix. Some platforms,
4762	notably the BSDs, can return more than six random characters, and replace
4763	trailing X characters in prefix with random characters.
4764	The optional options argument can be a string specifying an encoding, or an
4765	object with an encoding property specifying the character encoding to use.
4766	The created directory path is passed to the callback's second parameter. If
4767	encoding is 'buffer', then the resulting directory path is passed as a
4768	<Buffer>. Otherwise, the path is passed as a <string> using the specified
4769	encoding.
4770	import { mkdtemp } from 'node:fs';
4771	import { join } from 'node:path';
4772	import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
4773	
4774	mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'foo-'), (err, directory) => {
4775	if (err) throw err;
4776	console.log(directory);
4777	// Prints: /tmp/foo-itXde2 or C:\Users\...\AppData\Local\Temp\foo-itXde2
4778	}); copy
4779	The fs.mkdtemp() method will append the six randomly selected characters
4780	directly to the prefix string. For instance, given a directory /tmp, if the
4781	intention is to create a temporary directory within /tmp, the prefix
4782	must end with a trailing platform-specific path separator
4783	(require('node:path').sep).
4784	import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
4785	import { mkdtemp } from 'node:fs';
4786	
4787	// The parent directory for the new temporary directory
4788	const tmpDir = tmpdir();
4789	
4790	// This method is *INCORRECT*:
4791	mkdtemp(tmpDir, (err, directory) => {
4792	if (err) throw err;
4793	console.log(directory);
4794	// Will print something similar to `/tmpabc123`.
4795	// A new temporary directory is created at the file system root
4796	// rather than *within* the /tmp directory.
4797	});
4798	
4799	// This method is *CORRECT*:
4800	import { sep } from 'node:path';
4801	mkdtemp(`${tmpDir}${sep}`, (err, directory) => {
4802	if (err) throw err;
4803	console.log(directory);
4804	// Will print something similar to `/tmp/abc123`.
4805	// A new temporary directory is created within
4806	// the /tmp directory.
4807	}); copy
4808	
4809	fs.open(path[, flags[, mode]], callback)#
4810

4826	
4827	
4828	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
4829	flags <string> | <number> See support of file system flags.
4830	Default: 'r'.
4831	mode <string> | <integer> Default: 0o666 (readable and writable)
4832	callback <Function>
4833

4838	
4839	Asynchronous file open. See the POSIX open(2) documentation for more details.
4840	mode sets the file mode (permission and sticky bits), but only if the file was
4841	created. On Windows, only the write permission can be manipulated; see
4842	fs.chmod().

4845	by Naming Files, Paths, and Namespaces. Under NTFS, if the filename contains
4846	a colon, Node.js will open a file system stream, as described by
4847	this MSDN page.
4848	Functions based on fs.open() exhibit this behavior as well:
4849	fs.writeFile(), fs.readFile(), etc.

4851	fs.openAsBlob(path[, options])#
4852	
4853	History

4858	v19.8.0

4864	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
4865	options <Object>
4866	
4867	type <string> An optional mime type for the blob.
4868	
4869	
4870	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with a <Blob> upon success.
4871	
4872	Returns a <Blob> whose data is backed by the given file.
4873	The file must not be modified after the <Blob> is created. Any modifications
4874	will cause reading the <Blob> data to fail with a DOMException error.
4875	Synchronous stat operations on the file when the Blob is created, and before
4876	each read in order to detect whether the file data has been modified on disk.
4877	
4878	import { openAsBlob } from 'node:fs';
4879	
4880	const blob = await openAsBlob('the.file.txt');
4881	const ab = await blob.arrayBuffer();
4882	blob.stream();const { openAsBlob } = require('node:fs');
4883	
4884	(async () => {
4885	const blob = await openAsBlob('the.file.txt');
4886	const ab = await blob.arrayBuffer();
4887	blob.stream();
4888	})();copy

4931	fs.read(fd, buffer, offset, length, position, callback)#
4932	
4933	History
4934	
4935	VersionChanges
4936	v18.0.0
4937	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
4938	v10.10.0
4939	The buffer parameter can now be any TypedArray, or a DataView.
4940	v7.4.0
4941	The buffer parameter can now be a Uint8Array.
4942	v6.0.0
4943	The length parameter can now be 0.
4944	v0.0.2
4945	Added in: v0.0.2
4946	
4947	
4948	
4949	
4950	fd <integer>
4951	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> The buffer that the data will be
4952	written to.
4953	offset <integer> The position in buffer to write the data to.
4954	length <integer> The number of bytes to read.
4955	position <integer> | <bigint> | <null> Specifies where to begin reading from in the
4956	file. If position is null or -1 , data will be read from the current
4957	file position, and the file position will be updated. If position is
4958	a non-negative integer, the file position will be unchanged.
4959	callback <Function>
4960	
4961	err <Error>
4962	bytesRead <integer>
4963	buffer <Buffer>
4964	
4965	
4966	
4967	Read data from the file specified by fd.
4968	The callback is given the three arguments, (err, bytesRead, buffer).
4969	If the file is not modified concurrently, the end-of-file is reached when the
4970	number of bytes read is zero.
4971	If this method is invoked as its util.promisify()ed version, it returns
4972	a promise for an Object with bytesRead and buffer properties.
4973	The fs.read() method reads data from the file specified
4974	by the file descriptor (fd).
4975	The length argument indicates the maximum number
4976	of bytes that Node.js
4977	will attempt to read from the kernel.
4978	However, the actual number of bytes read (bytesRead) can be lower
4979	than the specified length for various reasons.
4980	For example:
4981	
4982	If the file is shorter than the specified length, bytesRead
4983	will be set to the actual number of bytes read.
4984	If the file encounters EOF (End of File) before the buffer could
4985	be filled, Node.js will read all available bytes until EOF is encountered,
4986	and the bytesRead parameter in the callback will indicate
4987	the actual number of bytes read, which may be less than the specified length.
4988	If the file is on a slow network filesystem
4989	or encounters any other issue during reading,
4990	bytesRead can be lower than the specified length.
4991	
4992	Therefore, when using fs.read(), it's important to
4993	check the bytesRead value to
4994	determine how many bytes were actually read from the file.
4995	Depending on your application
4996	logic, you may need to handle cases where bytesRead
4997	is lower than the specified length,
4998	such as by wrapping the read call in a loop if you require
4999	a minimum amount of bytes.
5000	This behavior is similar to the POSIX preadv2 function.
5001

5116	fs.readFile(path[, options], callback)#
5117	
5118	History
5119	
5120	VersionChanges
5121	v24.19.0
5122	Added support for the buffer option.
5123	v18.0.0
5124	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
5125	v16.0.0
5126	The error returned may be an AggregateError if more than one error is returned.
5127	v15.2.0, v14.17.0
5128	The options argument may include an AbortSignal to abort an ongoing readFile request.
5129	v10.0.0
5130	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will throw a TypeError at runtime.
5131	v7.6.0
5132	The path parameter can be a WHATWG URL object using file: protocol.
5133	v7.0.0
5134	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will emit a deprecation warning with id DEP0013.
5135	v5.1.0
5136	The callback will always be called with null as the error parameter in case of success.
5137	v5.0.0
5138	The path parameter can be a file descriptor now.
5139	v0.1.29
5140	Added in: v0.1.29
5141	
5142	
5143	
5144	
5145	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> | <integer> filename or file descriptor
5146	options <Object> | <string>
5147	
5148	encoding <string> | <null> Default: null
5149	flag <string> See support of file system flags. Default: 'r'.
5150	signal <AbortSignal> allows aborting an in-progress readFile
5151	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> | <Function> A buffer to read into, or a
5152	function called with the file size that returns the buffer.
5153	
5154	
5155	callback <Function>
5156	
5157	err <Error> | <AggregateError>
5158	data <string> | <Buffer>
5159	
5160	
5161	
5162	Asynchronously reads the entire contents of a file.
5163	import { readFile } from 'node:fs';
5164	
5165	readFile('/etc/passwd', (err, data) => {
5166	if (err) throw err;
5167	console.log(data);
5168	}); copy
5169	The callback is passed two arguments (err, data), where data is the
5170	contents of the file.
5171	If no encoding is specified, then the raw buffer is returned.
5172	If buffer is provided and no encoding is specified, the returned <Buffer> is
5173	a view over the supplied buffer containing only the bytes read. If the
5174	supplied buffer is too small to contain the entire file, the callback is
5175	called with an error.
5176	If options is a string, then it specifies the encoding:
5177	import { readFile } from 'node:fs';
5178	
5179	readFile('/etc/passwd', 'utf8', callback); copy
5180	When the path is a directory, the behavior of fs.readFile() and
5181	fs.readFileSync() is platform-specific. On macOS, Linux, and Windows, an
5182	error will be returned. On FreeBSD, a representation of the directory's contents
5183	will be returned.
5184	import { readFile } from 'node:fs';
5185	
5186	// macOS, Linux, and Windows
5187	readFile('<directory>', (err, data) => {
5188	// => [Error: EISDIR: illegal operation on a directory, read <directory>]
5189	});
5190	
5191	// FreeBSD
5192	readFile('<directory>', (err, data) => {
5193	// => null, <data>
5194	}); copy
5195	It is possible to abort an ongoing request using an AbortSignal. If a
5196	request is aborted the callback is called with an AbortError:
5197	import { readFile } from 'node:fs';
5198	
5199	const controller = new AbortController();
5200	const signal = controller.signal;
5201	readFile(fileInfo[0].name, { signal }, (err, buf) => {
5202	// ...
5203	});
5204	// When you want to abort the request
5205	controller.abort(); copy
5206	The fs.readFile() function buffers the entire file. To minimize memory costs,
5207	when possible prefer streaming via fs.createReadStream().
5208	Aborting an ongoing request does not abort individual operating
5209	system requests but rather the internal buffering fs.readFile performs.
5210	An example using the buffer option with a pre-allocated buffer:
5211	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
5212	import { readFile } from 'node:fs';
5213	
5214	const buf = Buffer.alloc(16384);
5215	readFile('/path/to/file', { buffer: buf }, (err, data) => {
5216	if (err) throw err;
5217	console.log(data); // A view over `buf` containing only the bytes read
5218	}); copy
5219	An example using the buffer option with a function returning a buffer:
5220	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
5221	import { readFile } from 'node:fs';
5222	
5223	readFile('/path/to/file', {
5224	buffer: (size) => Buffer.alloc(size),
5225	}, (err, data) => {
5226	if (err) throw err;
5227	console.log(data);
5228	}); copy
5229	
5230	File descriptors#
5231	
5232	Any specified file descriptor has to support reading.
5233	If a file descriptor is specified as the path, it will not be closed
5234	automatically.
5235	The reading will begin at the current position. For example, if the file
5236	already had 'Hello World' and six bytes are read with the file descriptor,
5237	the call to fs.readFile() with the same file descriptor, would give
5238	'World', rather than 'Hello World'.
5239	
5240	
5241	Performance Considerations#
5242	The fs.readFile() method asynchronously reads the contents of a file into
5243	memory one chunk at a time, allowing the event loop to turn between each chunk.
5244	This allows the read operation to have less impact on other activity that may
5245	be using the underlying libuv thread pool but means that it will take longer
5246	to read a complete file into memory.
5247	The additional read overhead can vary broadly on different systems and depends
5248	on the type of file being read. If the file type is not a regular file (a pipe
5249	for instance) and Node.js is unable to determine an actual file size, each read
5250	operation will load on 64 KiB of data. For regular files, each read will process
5251	512 KiB of data.
5252	For applications that require as-fast-as-possible reading of file contents, it
5253	is better to use fs.read() directly and for application code to manage
5254	reading the full contents of the file itself.
5255	The Node.js GitHub issue #25741 provides more information and a detailed
5256	analysis on the performance of fs.readFile() for multiple file sizes in
5257	different Node.js versions.
5258

5333	fs.realpath(path[, options], callback)#
5334	
5335	History
5336	
5337	VersionChanges
5338	v18.0.0
5339	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
5340	v10.0.0
5341	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will throw a TypeError at runtime.
5342	v8.0.0
5343	Pipe/Socket resolve support was added.
5344	v7.6.0
5345	The path parameter can be a WHATWG URL object using file: protocol.
5346	v7.0.0
5347	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will emit a deprecation warning with id DEP0013.
5348	v6.4.0
5349	Calling realpath now works again for various edge cases on Windows.
5350	v6.0.0
5351	The cache parameter was removed.
5352	v0.1.31
5353	Added in: v0.1.31
5354	
5355	
5356	
5357	
5358	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
5359	options <string> | <Object>
5360	
5361	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
5362	
5363	
5364	callback <Function>
5365	
5366	err <Error>
5367	resolvedPath <string> | <Buffer>
5368	
5369	
5370	
5371	Asynchronously computes the canonical pathname by resolving ., .., and
5372	symbolic links.
5373	A canonical pathname is not necessarily unique. Hard links and bind mounts can
5374	expose a file system entity through many pathnames.
5375	This function behaves like realpath(3), with some exceptions:
5376	
5377	
5378	No case conversion is performed on case-insensitive file systems.
5379	
5380	
5381	The maximum number of symbolic links is platform-independent and generally
5382	(much) higher than what the native realpath(3) implementation supports.
5383	
5384	
5385	The callback gets two arguments (err, resolvedPath). May use process.cwd
5386	to resolve relative paths.
5387	Only paths that can be converted to UTF8 strings are supported.
5388	The optional options argument can be a string specifying an encoding, or an
5389	object with an encoding property specifying the character encoding to use for
5390	the path passed to the callback. If the encoding is set to 'buffer',
5391	the path returned will be passed as a <Buffer> object.
5392	If path resolves to a socket or a pipe, the function will return a system
5393	dependent name for that object.
5394	A path that does not exist results in an ENOENT error.
5395	error.path is the absolute file path.
5396

5409	
5410	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
5411	options <string> | <Object>
5412	
5413	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
5414	
5415	
5416	callback <Function>
5417	
5418	err <Error>
5419	resolvedPath <string> | <Buffer>
5420	
5421	
5422	
5423	Asynchronous realpath(3).
5424	The callback gets two arguments (err, resolvedPath).
5425	Only paths that can be converted to UTF8 strings are supported.
5426	The optional options argument can be a string specifying an encoding, or an
5427	object with an encoding property specifying the character encoding to use for
5428	the path passed to the callback. If the encoding is set to 'buffer',
5429	the path returned will be passed as a <Buffer> object.
5430	On Linux, when Node.js is linked against musl libc, the procfs file system must
5431	be mounted on /proc in order for this function to work. Glibc does not have
5432	this restriction.

5491	v12.10.0

5506	options <Object>
5507	
5508	maxRetries <integer> If an EBUSY, EMFILE, ENFILE, ENOTEMPTY, or
5509	EPERM error is encountered, Node.js retries the operation with a linear
5510	backoff wait of retryDelay milliseconds longer on each try. This option
5511	represents the number of retries. This option is ignored if the recursive
5512	option is not true. Default: 0.
5513	recursive <boolean> If true, perform a recursive directory removal. In
5514	recursive mode, operations are retried on failure. Default: false.
5515	Deprecated.
5516	retryDelay <integer> The amount of time in milliseconds to wait between
5517	retries. This option is ignored if the recursive option is not true.
5518	Default: 100.

5614	Asynchronous stat(2). The callback gets two arguments (err, stats) where
5615	stats is an <fs.Stats> object.
5616	In case of an error, the err.code will be one of Common System Errors.
5617	fs.stat() follows symbolic links. Use fs.lstat() to look at the
5618	links themselves.
5619	Using fs.stat() to check for the existence of a file before calling
5620	fs.open(), fs.readFile(), or fs.writeFile() is not recommended.
5621	Instead, user code should open/read/write the file directly and handle the
5622	error raised if the file is not available.

5624	is recommended.

5630	import { stat } from 'node:fs';

5633	
5634	for (let i = 0; i < pathsToCheck.length; i++) {
5635	stat(pathsToCheck[i], (err, stats) => {
5636	console.log(stats.isDirectory());
5637	console.log(stats);
5638	});
5639	} copy

5708	fs.symlink(target, path[, type], callback)#
5709	
5710	History
5711	
5712	VersionChanges
5713	v18.0.0
5714	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
5715	v12.0.0
5716	If the type argument is left undefined, Node will autodetect target type and automatically select dir or file.
5717	v7.6.0
5718	The target and path parameters can be WHATWG URL objects using file: protocol. Support is currently still experimental.
5719	v0.1.31
5720	Added in: v0.1.31
5721	
5722	
5723	
5724	
5725	target <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
5726	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
5727	type <string> | <null> Default: null
5728	callback <Function>
5729	
5730	err <Error>
5731	
5732	
5733	
5734	Creates the link called path pointing to target. No arguments other than a
5735	possible exception are given to the completion callback.
5736	See the POSIX symlink(2) documentation for more details.
5737	The type argument is only available on Windows and ignored on other platforms.
5738	It can be set to 'dir', 'file', or 'junction'. If the type argument is
5739	null, Node.js will autodetect target type and use 'file' or 'dir'.
5740	If the target does not exist, 'file' will be used. Windows junction points
5741	require the destination path to be absolute. When using 'junction', the
5742	target argument will automatically be normalized to absolute path. Junction
5743	points on NTFS volumes can only point to directories.
5744	Relative targets are relative to the link's parent directory.
5745	import { symlink } from 'node:fs';
5746	
5747	symlink('./mew', './mewtwo', callback); copy
5748	The above example creates a symbolic link mewtwo which points to mew in the
5749	same directory:
5750	$ tree .
5751	.
5752	├── mew
5753	└── mewtwo -> ./mew copy

5839	fs.unwatchFile(filename[, listener])#
5840	
5841	Added in: v0.1.31
5842	
5843	
5844	filename <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
5845	listener <Function> Optional, a listener previously attached using
5846	fs.watchFile()
5847	
5848	Stop watching for changes on filename. If listener is specified, only that
5849	particular listener is removed. Otherwise, all listeners are removed,
5850	effectively stopping watching of filename.
5851	Calling fs.unwatchFile() with a filename that is not being watched is a
5852	no-op, not an error.
5853	Using fs.watch() is more efficient than fs.watchFile() and
5854	fs.unwatchFile(). fs.watch() should be used instead of fs.watchFile()
5855	and fs.unwatchFile() when possible.

5898	fs.watch(filename[, options][, listener])#
5899	
5900	History
5901	
5902	VersionChanges
5903	v24.16.0
5904	Added throwIfNoEntry option.
5905	v19.1.0
5906	Added recursive support for Linux, AIX and IBMi.
5907	v15.9.0, v14.17.0
5908	Added support for closing the watcher with an AbortSignal.
5909	v7.6.0
5910	The filename parameter can be a WHATWG URL object using file: protocol.
5911	v7.0.0
5912	The passed options object will never be modified.
5913	v0.5.10
5914	Added in: v0.5.10
5915	
5916	
5917	
5918	
5919	filename <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
5920	options <string> | <Object>
5921	
5922	persistent <boolean> Indicates whether the process should continue to run
5923	as long as files are being watched. Default: true.
5924	recursive <boolean> Indicates whether all subdirectories should be
5925	watched, or only the current directory. This applies when a directory is
5926	specified, and only on supported platforms (See caveats). Default:
5927	false.
5928	encoding <string> Specifies the character encoding to be used for the
5929	filename passed to the listener. Default: 'utf8'.
5930	signal <AbortSignal> allows closing the watcher with an AbortSignal.
5931	throwIfNoEntry <boolean> Indicates whether an exception should be thrown when the
5932	path does not exist. Default: true.
5933	ignore <string> | <RegExp> | <Function> | <Array> Pattern(s) to ignore. Strings are
5934	glob patterns (using minimatch), RegExp patterns are tested against
5935	the filename, and functions receive the filename and return true to
5936	ignore. Default: undefined.
5937	
5938	
5939	listener <Function> | <undefined> Default: undefined
5940	
5941	eventType <string>
5942	filename <string> | <Buffer> | <null>
5943	
5944	
5945	Returns: <fs.FSWatcher>
5946	
5947	Watch for changes on filename, where filename is either a file or a
5948	directory.
5949	The second argument is optional. If options is provided as a string, it
5950	specifies the encoding. Otherwise options should be passed as an object.
5951	The listener callback gets two arguments (eventType, filename). eventType
5952	is either 'rename' or 'change', and filename is the name of the file
5953	which triggered the event.
5954	On most platforms, 'rename' is emitted whenever a filename appears or
5955	disappears in the directory.
5956	The listener callback is attached to the 'change' event fired by
5957	<fs.FSWatcher>, but it is not the same thing as the 'change' value of
5958	eventType.
5959	If a signal is passed, aborting the corresponding AbortController will close
5960	the returned <fs.FSWatcher>.
5961	
5962	Caveats#
5963	
5964	The fs.watch API is not 100% consistent across platforms, and is
5965	unavailable in some situations.
5966	On Windows, no events will be emitted if the watched directory is moved or
5967	renamed. An EPERM error is reported when the watched directory is deleted.
5968	The fs.watch API does not provide any protection with respect
5969	to malicious actions on the file system. For example, on Windows it is
5970	implemented by monitoring changes in a directory versus specific files. This
5971	allows substitution of a file and fs reporting changes on the new file
5972	with the same filename.
5973	
5974	Availability#
5975	
5976	This feature depends on the underlying operating system providing a way
5977	to be notified of file system changes.
5978	
5979	On Linux systems, this uses inotify(7).
5980	On BSD systems, this uses kqueue(2).
5981	On macOS, this uses kqueue(2) for files and FSEvents for
5982	directories.
5983	On SunOS systems (including Solaris and SmartOS), this uses event ports.
5984	On Windows systems, this feature depends on ReadDirectoryChangesW.
5985	On AIX systems, this feature depends on AHAFS, which must be enabled.
5986	On IBM i systems, this feature is not supported.
5987	
5988	If the underlying functionality is not available for some reason, then
5989	fs.watch() will not be able to function and may throw an exception.
5990	For example, watching files or directories can be unreliable, and in some
5991	cases impossible, on network file systems (NFS, SMB, etc) or host file systems
5992	when using virtualization software such as Vagrant or Docker.
5993	It is still possible to use fs.watchFile(), which uses stat polling, but
5994	this method is slower and less reliable.
5995	
5996	Inodes#
5997	
5998	On Linux and macOS systems, fs.watch() resolves the path to an inode and
5999	watches the inode. If the watched path is deleted and recreated, it is assigned
6000	a new inode. The watch will emit an event for the delete but will continue
6001	watching the original inode. Events for the new inode will not be emitted.
6002	This is expected behavior.
6003	AIX files retain the same inode for the lifetime of a file. Saving and closing a
6004	watched file on AIX will result in two notifications (one for adding new
6005	content, and one for truncation).
6006	
6007	Filename argument#
6008	
6009	Providing filename argument in the callback is only supported on Linux,
6010	macOS, Windows, and AIX. Even on supported platforms, filename is not always
6011	guaranteed to be provided. Therefore, don't assume that filename argument is
6012	always provided in the callback, and have some fallback logic if it is null.
6013	import { watch } from 'node:fs';
6014	watch('somedir', (eventType, filename) => {
6015	console.log(`event type is: ${eventType}`);
6016	if (filename) {
6017	console.log(`filename provided: ${filename}`);
6018	} else {
6019	console.log('filename not provided');
6020	}
6021	}); copy
6022	
6023	fs.watchFile(filename[, options], listener)#
6024	
6025	History
6026	
6027	VersionChanges
6028	v10.5.0
6029	The bigint option is now supported.
6030	v7.6.0
6031	The filename parameter can be a WHATWG URL object using file: protocol.
6032	v0.1.31
6033	Added in: v0.1.31
6034	
6035	
6036	
6037	
6038	filename <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
6039	options <Object>
6040	
6041	bigint <boolean> Default: false
6042	persistent <boolean> Default: true
6043	interval <integer> Default: 5007
6044	
6045	
6046	listener <Function>
6047	
6048	current <fs.Stats>
6049	previous <fs.Stats>
6050	
6051	
6052	Returns: <fs.StatWatcher>
6053	
6054	Watch for changes on filename. The callback listener will be called each
6055	time the file is accessed.
6056	The options argument may be omitted. If provided, it should be an object. The
6057	options object may contain a boolean named persistent that indicates
6058	whether the process should continue to run as long as files are being watched.
6059	The options object may specify an interval property indicating how often the
6060	target should be polled in milliseconds.
6061	The listener gets two arguments the current stat object and the previous
6062	stat object:
6063	import { watchFile } from 'node:fs';
6064	
6065	watchFile('message.text', (curr, prev) => {
6066	console.log(`the current mtime is: ${curr.mtime}`);
6067	console.log(`the previous mtime was: ${prev.mtime}`);
6068	}); copy
6069	These stat objects are instances of fs.Stat. If the bigint option is true,
6070	the numeric values in these objects are specified as BigInt s.
6071	To be notified when the file was modified, not just accessed, it is necessary
6072	to compare curr.mtimeMs and prev.mtimeMs.
6073	When an fs.watchFile operation results in an ENOENT error, it
6074	will invoke the listener once, with all the fields zeroed (or, for dates, the
6075	Unix Epoch). If the file is created later on, the listener will be called
6076	again, with the latest stat objects. This is a change in functionality since
6077	v0.10.
6078	Using fs.watch() is more efficient than fs.watchFile and
6079	fs.unwatchFile. fs.watch should be used instead of fs.watchFile and
6080	fs.unwatchFile when possible.
6081	When a file being watched by fs.watchFile() disappears and reappears,
6082	then the contents of previous in the second callback event (the file's
6083	reappearance) will be the same as the contents of previous in the first
6084	callback event (its disappearance).
6085	This happens when:
6086	
6087	the file is deleted, followed by a restore
6088	the file is renamed and then renamed a second time back to its original name
6089	
6090	
6091	fs.write(fd, buffer, offset[, length[, position]], callback)#
6092	
6093	History
6094	
6095	VersionChanges
6096	v18.0.0
6097	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
6098	v14.0.0
6099	The buffer parameter won't coerce unsupported input to strings anymore.
6100	v10.10.0
6101	The buffer parameter can now be any TypedArray or a DataView.
6102	v10.0.0
6103	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will throw a TypeError at runtime.
6104	v7.4.0
6105	The buffer parameter can now be a Uint8Array.
6106	v7.2.0
6107	The offset and length parameters are optional now.
6108	v7.0.0
6109	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will emit a deprecation warning with id DEP0013.
6110	v0.0.2
6111	Added in: v0.0.2
6112	
6113	
6114	
6115	
6116	fd <integer>
6117	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView>
6118	offset <integer> Default: 0
6119	length <integer> Default: buffer.byteLength - offset
6120	position <integer> | <null> Default: null
6121	callback <Function>
6122	
6123	err <Error>
6124	bytesWritten <integer>
6125	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView>
6126	
6127	
6128	
6129	Write buffer to the file specified by fd.
6130	offset determines the part of the buffer to be written, and length is
6131	an integer specifying the number of bytes to write.
6132	position refers to the offset from the beginning of the file where this data
6133	should be written. If typeof position !== 'number', the data will be written
6134	at the current position. See pwrite(2).
6135	The callback will be given three arguments (err, bytesWritten, buffer) where
6136	bytesWritten specifies how many bytes were written from buffer.
6137	If this method is invoked as its util.promisify()ed version, it returns
6138	a promise for an Object with bytesWritten and buffer properties.
6139	It is unsafe to use fs.write() multiple times on the same file without waiting
6140	for the callback. For this scenario, fs.createWriteStream() is
6141	recommended.
6142	On Linux, positional writes don't work when the file is opened in append mode.
6143	The kernel ignores the position argument and always appends the data to
6144	the end of the file.
6145	
6146	fs.write(fd, buffer[, options], callback)#
6147	
6148	Added in: v18.3.0, v16.17.0
6149	
6150	
6151	fd <integer>
6152	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView>
6153	options <Object>
6154	
6155	offset <integer> Default: 0
6156	length <integer> Default: buffer.byteLength - offset
6157	position <integer> | <null> Default: null
6158	
6159	
6160	callback <Function>
6161	
6162	err <Error>
6163	bytesWritten <integer>
6164	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView>
6165	
6166	
6167	
6168	Write buffer to the file specified by fd.
6169	Similar to the above fs.write function, this version takes an
6170	optional options object. If no options object is specified, it will
6171	default with the above values.
6172	
6173	fs.write(fd, string[, position[, encoding]], callback)#
6174	
6175	History
6176	
6177	VersionChanges
6178	v19.0.0
6179	Passing to the string parameter an object with an own toString function is no longer supported.
6180	v17.8.0
6181	Passing to the string parameter an object with an own toString function is deprecated.
6182	v14.12.0
6183	The string parameter will stringify an object with an explicit toString function.
6184	v14.0.0
6185	The string parameter won't coerce unsupported input to strings anymore.
6186	v10.0.0
6187	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will throw a TypeError at runtime.
6188	v7.2.0
6189	The position parameter is optional now.
6190	v7.0.0
6191	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will emit a deprecation warning with id DEP0013.
6192	v0.11.5
6193	Added in: v0.11.5
6194	
6195	
6196	
6197	
6198	fd <integer>
6199	string <string>
6200	position <integer> | <null> Default: null
6201	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
6202	callback <Function>
6203	
6204	err <Error>
6205	written <integer>
6206	string <string>
6207	
6208	
6209	
6210	Write string to the file specified by fd. If string is not a string,
6211	an exception is thrown.
6212	position refers to the offset from the beginning of the file where this data
6213	should be written. If typeof position !== 'number' the data will be written at
6214	the current position. See pwrite(2).
6215	encoding is the expected string encoding.
6216	The callback will receive the arguments (err, written, string) where written
6217	specifies how many bytes the passed string required to be written. Bytes
6218	written is not necessarily the same as string characters written. See
6219	Buffer.byteLength.
6220	It is unsafe to use fs.write() multiple times on the same file without waiting
6221	for the callback. For this scenario, fs.createWriteStream() is
6222	recommended.
6223	On Linux, positional writes don't work when the file is opened in append mode.
6224	The kernel ignores the position argument and always appends the data to
6225	the end of the file.
6226	On Windows, if the file descriptor is connected to the console (e.g. fd == 1
6227	or stdout) a string containing non-ASCII characters will not be rendered
6228	properly by default, regardless of the encoding used.
6229	It is possible to configure the console to render UTF-8 properly by changing the
6230	active codepage with the chcp 65001 command. See the chcp docs for more
6231	details.
6232	
6233	fs.writeFile(file, data[, options], callback)#
6234	
6235	History
6236	
6237	VersionChanges
6238	v21.0.0, v20.10.0
6239	The flush option is now supported.
6240	v19.0.0
6241	Passing to the string parameter an object with an own toString function is no longer supported.
6242	v18.0.0
6243	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
6244	v17.8.0
6245	Passing to the string parameter an object with an own toString function is deprecated.
6246	v16.0.0
6247	The error returned may be an AggregateError if more than one error is returned.
6248	v15.2.0, v14.17.0
6249	The options argument may include an AbortSignal to abort an ongoing writeFile request.
6250	v14.12.0
6251	The data parameter will stringify an object with an explicit toString function.
6252	v14.0.0
6253	The data parameter won't coerce unsupported input to strings anymore.
6254	v10.10.0
6255	The data parameter can now be any TypedArray or a DataView.
6256	v10.0.0
6257	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will throw a TypeError at runtime.
6258	v7.4.0
6259	The data parameter can now be a Uint8Array.
6260	v7.0.0
6261	The callback parameter is no longer optional. Not passing it will emit a deprecation warning with id DEP0013.
6262	v5.0.0
6263	The file parameter can be a file descriptor now.
6264	v0.1.29
6265	Added in: v0.1.29
6266	
6267	
6268	
6269	
6270	file <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> | <integer> filename or file descriptor
6271	data <string> | <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView>
6272	options <Object> | <string>
6273	
6274	encoding <string> | <null> Default: 'utf8'
6275	mode <integer> Default: 0o666
6276	flag <string> See support of file system flags. Default: 'w'.
6277	flush <boolean> If all data is successfully written to the file, and
6278	flush is true, fs.fsync() is used to flush the data.
6279	Default: false.
6280	signal <AbortSignal> allows aborting an in-progress writeFile
6281	
6282	
6283	callback <Function>
6284	
6285	err <Error> | <AggregateError>
6286	
6287	
6288	
6289	When file is a filename, asynchronously writes data to the file, replacing the
6290	file if it already exists. data can be a string or a buffer.
6291	When file is a file descriptor, the behavior is similar to calling
6292	fs.write() directly (which is recommended). See the notes below on using
6293	a file descriptor.
6294	The encoding option is ignored if data is a buffer.
6295	The mode option only affects the newly created file. See fs.open()
6296	for more details.
6297	import { writeFile } from 'node:fs';
6298	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
6299	
6300	const data = new Uint8Array(Buffer.from('Hello Node.js'));
6301	writeFile('message.txt', data, (err) => {
6302	if (err) throw err;
6303	console.log('The file has been saved!');
6304	}); copy
6305	If options is a string, then it specifies the encoding:
6306	import { writeFile } from 'node:fs';
6307	
6308	writeFile('message.txt', 'Hello Node.js', 'utf8', callback); copy
6309	It is unsafe to use fs.writeFile() multiple times on the same file without
6310	waiting for the callback. For this scenario, fs.createWriteStream() is
6311	recommended.
6312	Similarly to fs.readFile - fs.writeFile is a convenience method that
6313	performs multiple write calls internally to write the buffer passed to it.
6314	For performance sensitive code consider using fs.createWriteStream().
6315	It is possible to use an <AbortSignal> to cancel an fs.writeFile().
6316	Cancelation is "best effort", and some amount of data is likely still
6317	to be written.
6318	import { writeFile } from 'node:fs';
6319	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
6320	
6321	const controller = new AbortController();
6322	const { signal } = controller;
6323	const data = new Uint8Array(Buffer.from('Hello Node.js'));
6324	writeFile('message.txt', data, { signal }, (err) => {
6325	// When a request is aborted - the callback is called with an AbortError
6326	});
6327	// When the request should be aborted
6328	controller.abort(); copy
6329	Aborting an ongoing request does not abort individual operating
6330	system requests but rather the internal buffering fs.writeFile performs.
6331	
6332	Using fs.writeFile() with file descriptors#
6333	When file is a file descriptor, the behavior is almost identical to directly
6334	calling fs.write() like:
6335	import { write } from 'node:fs';
6336	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
6337	
6338	write(fd, Buffer.from(data, options.encoding), callback); copy
6339	The difference from directly calling fs.write() is that under some unusual
6340	conditions, fs.write() might write only part of the buffer and need to be
6341	retried to write the remaining data, whereas fs.writeFile() retries until
6342	the data is entirely written (or an error occurs).
6343	The implications of this are a common source of confusion. In
6344	the file descriptor case, the file is not replaced! The data is not necessarily
6345	written to the beginning of the file, and the file's original data may remain
6346	before and/or after the newly written data.
6347	For example, if fs.writeFile() is called twice in a row, first to write the
6348	string 'Hello', then to write the string ', World', the file would contain
6349	'Hello, World', and might contain some of the file's original data (depending
6350	on the size of the original file, and the position of the file descriptor). If
6351	a file name had been used instead of a descriptor, the file would be guaranteed
6352	to contain only ', World'.
6353	
6354	fs.writev(fd, buffers[, position], callback)#
6355	
6356	History
6357	
6358	VersionChanges
6359	v18.0.0
6360	Passing an invalid callback to the callback argument now throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE instead of ERR_INVALID_CALLBACK.
6361	v12.9.0
6362	Added in: v12.9.0
6363	
6364	
6365	
6366	
6367	fd <integer>
6368	buffers <ArrayBufferView[]>
6369	position <integer> | <null> Default: null
6370	callback <Function>
6371	
6372	err <Error>
6373	bytesWritten <integer>
6374	buffers <ArrayBufferView[]>
6375	
6376	
6377	
6378	Write an array of ArrayBufferView s to the file specified by fd using
6379	writev().
6380	position is the offset from the beginning of the file where this data
6381	should be written. If typeof position !== 'number', the data will be written
6382	at the current position.
6383	The callback will be given three arguments: err, bytesWritten, and
6384	buffers. bytesWritten is how many bytes were written from buffers.
6385	If this method is util.promisify()ed, it returns a promise for an
6386	Object with bytesWritten and buffers properties.
6387	It is unsafe to use fs.writev() multiple times on the same file without
6388	waiting for the callback. For this scenario, use fs.createWriteStream().
6389	On Linux, positional writes don't work when the file is opened in append mode.
6390	The kernel ignores the position argument and always appends the data to
6391	the end of the file.
6392	
6393	Synchronous API#
6394	The synchronous APIs perform all operations synchronously, blocking the
6395	event loop until the operation completes or fails.
6396	
6397	fs.accessSync(path[, mode])#
6398	
6399	History
6400	
6401	VersionChanges
6402	v7.6.0
6403	The path parameter can be a WHATWG URL object using file: protocol.
6404	v0.11.15
6405	Added in: v0.11.15
6406	
6407	
6408	
6409	
6410	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
6411	mode <integer> Default: fs.constants.F_OK
6412	
6413	Synchronously tests a user's permissions for the file or directory specified
6414	by path. The mode argument is an optional integer that specifies the
6415	accessibility checks to be performed. mode should be either the value
6416	fs.constants.F_OK or a mask consisting of the bitwise OR of any of
6417	fs.constants.R_OK, fs.constants.W_OK, and fs.constants.X_OK (e.g.
6418	fs.constants.W_OK | fs.constants.R_OK). Check File access constants for
6419	possible values of mode.
6420	If any of the accessibility checks fail, an Error will be thrown. Otherwise,
6421	the method will return undefined.
6422	import { accessSync, constants } from 'node:fs';
6423	
6424	try {
6425	accessSync('etc/passwd', constants.R_OK | constants.W_OK);
6426	console.log('can read/write');
6427	} catch (err) {
6428	console.error('no access!');
6429	} copy
6430	
6431	fs.appendFileSync(path, data[, options])#
6432	
6433	History
6434	
6435	VersionChanges
6436	v21.1.0, v20.10.0
6437	The flush option is now supported.
6438	v7.0.0
6439	The passed options object will never be modified.
6440	v5.0.0
6441	The file parameter can be a file descriptor now.
6442	v0.6.7
6443	Added in: v0.6.7
6444	
6445	
6446	
6447	
6448	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> | <number> filename or file descriptor
6449	data <string> | <Buffer>
6450	options <Object> | <string>
6451	
6452	encoding <string> | <null> Default: 'utf8'
6453	mode <integer> Default: 0o666
6454	flag <string> See support of file system flags. Default: 'a'.
6455	flush <boolean> If true, the underlying file descriptor is flushed
6456	prior to closing it. Default: false.
6457	
6458	
6459	
6460	Synchronously append data to a file, creating the file if it does not yet
6461	exist. data can be a string or a <Buffer>.
6462	The mode option only affects the newly created file. See fs.open()
6463	for more details.
6464	import { appendFileSync } from 'node:fs';
6465	
6466	try {
6467	appendFileSync('message.txt', 'data to append');
6468	console.log('The "data to append" was appended to file!');
6469	} catch (err) {
6470	/* Handle the error */
6471	} copy
6472	If options is a string, then it specifies the encoding:
6473	import { appendFileSync } from 'node:fs';
6474	
6475	appendFileSync('message.txt', 'data to append', 'utf8'); copy
6476	The path may be specified as a numeric file descriptor that has been opened
6477	for appending (using fs.open() or fs.openSync()). The file descriptor will
6478	not be closed automatically.
6479	import { openSync, closeSync, appendFileSync } from 'node:fs';
6480	
6481	let fd;
6482	
6483	try {
6484	fd = openSync('message.txt', 'a');
6485	appendFileSync(fd, 'data to append', 'utf8');
6486	} catch (err) {
6487	/* Handle the error */
6488	} finally {
6489	if (fd !== undefined)
6490	closeSync(fd);
6491	} copy

6535	
6536	Added in: v0.1.21

6540	
6541	Closes the file descriptor. Returns undefined.

6543	through any other fs operation may lead to undefined behavior.
6544	See the POSIX close(2) documentation for more detail.

6546	fs.copyFileSync(src, dest[, mode])#
6547	
6548	History
6549	
6550	VersionChanges
6551	v14.0.0
6552	Changed flags argument to mode and imposed stricter type validation.
6553	v8.5.0
6554	Added in: v8.5.0
6555	
6556	
6557	
6558	
6559	src <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> source filename to copy
6560	dest <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> destination filename of the copy operation
6561	mode <integer> modifiers for copy operation. Default: 0.
6562	
6563	Synchronously copies src to dest. By default, dest is overwritten if it
6564	already exists. Returns undefined. Node.js makes no guarantees about the
6565	atomicity of the copy operation. If an error occurs after the destination file
6566	has been opened for writing, Node.js will attempt to remove the destination.
6567	Symbolic links are followed. If src is a symbolic link, the target file is
6568	copied. If dest is a symbolic link, the target file is overwritten unless
6569	mode contains fs.constants.COPYFILE_EXCL.
6570	mode is an optional integer that specifies the behavior
6571	of the copy operation. It is possible to create a mask consisting of the bitwise
6572	OR of two or more values (e.g.
6573	fs.constants.COPYFILE_EXCL | fs.constants.COPYFILE_FICLONE).
6574	
6575	fs.constants.COPYFILE_EXCL: The copy operation will fail if dest already
6576	exists.
6577	fs.constants.COPYFILE_FICLONE: The copy operation will attempt to create a
6578	copy-on-write reflink. If the platform does not support copy-on-write, then a
6579	fallback copy mechanism is used.
6580	fs.constants.COPYFILE_FICLONE_FORCE: The copy operation will attempt to
6581	create a copy-on-write reflink. If the platform does not support
6582	copy-on-write, then the operation will fail.
6583	
6584	import { copyFileSync, constants } from 'node:fs';
6585	
6586	// destination.txt will be created or overwritten by default.
6587	copyFileSync('source.txt', 'destination.txt');
6588	console.log('source.txt was copied to destination.txt');
6589	
6590	// By using COPYFILE_EXCL, the operation will fail if destination.txt exists.
6591	copyFileSync('source.txt', 'destination.txt', constants.COPYFILE_EXCL); copy
6592	
6593	fs.cpSync(src, dest[, options])#
6594	
6595	History
6596	
6597	VersionChanges
6598	v22.3.0
6599	This API is no longer experimental.
6600	v20.1.0, v18.17.0
6601	Accept an additional mode option to specify the copy behavior as the mode argument of fs.copyFile().
6602	v17.6.0, v16.15.0
6603	Accepts an additional verbatimSymlinks option to specify whether to perform path resolution for symlinks.
6604	v16.7.0
6605	Added in: v16.7.0
6606	
6607	
6608	
6609	
6610	src <string> | <URL> source path to copy.
6611	dest <string> | <URL> destination path to copy to.
6612	options <Object>
6613	
6614	dereference <boolean> dereference symlinks. Default: false.
6615	errorOnExist <boolean> when force is false, and the destination
6616	exists, throw an error. Default: false.
6617	filter <Function> Function to filter copied files/directories. Return
6618	true to copy the item, false to ignore it. When ignoring a directory,
6619	all of its contents will be skipped as well. Default: undefined
6620	
6621	src <string> source path to copy.
6622	dest <string> destination path to copy to.
6623	Returns: <boolean> Any non-Promise value that is coercible
6624	to boolean.
6625	
6626	
6627	force <boolean> overwrite existing file or directory. The copy
6628	operation will ignore errors if you set this to false and the destination
6629	exists. Use the errorOnExist option to change this behavior.
6630	Default: true.
6631	mode <integer> modifiers for copy operation. Default: 0.
6632	See mode flag of fs.copyFileSync().
6633	preserveTimestamps <boolean> When true timestamps from src will
6634	be preserved. Default: false.
6635	recursive <boolean> copy directories recursively Default: false
6636	verbatimSymlinks <boolean> When true, path resolution for symlinks will
6637	be skipped. Default: false
6638	
6639	
6640	
6641	Synchronously copies the entire directory structure from src to dest,
6642	including subdirectories and files.
6643	When copying a directory to another directory, globs are not supported and
6644	behavior is similar to cp dir1/ dir2/.
6645

6661	
6662	Returns true if the path exists, false otherwise.
6663	For detailed information, see the documentation of the asynchronous version of
6664	this API: fs.exists().
6665	fs.exists() is deprecated, but fs.existsSync() is not. The callback
6666	parameter to fs.exists() accepts parameters that are inconsistent with other
6667	Node.js callbacks. fs.existsSync() does not use a callback.
6668	import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';

6670	if (existsSync('/etc/passwd'))
6671	console.log('The path exists.'); copy

6978	fs.mkdtempDisposableSync(prefix[, options])#
6979	
6980	Added in: v24.4.0
6981	
6982	
6983	prefix <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
6984	options <string> | <Object>
6985	
6986	encoding <string> Default: 'utf8'
6987	
6988	
6989	Returns: <Object> A disposable object:
6990	
6991	path <string> | <Buffer> The path of the created directory.
6992	remove <Function> A function which removes the created directory.
6993	[Symbol.dispose] <Function> The same as remove.
6994	
6995	
6996	
6997	Returns a disposable object whose path property holds the created directory
6998	path. If encoding is 'buffer', the path will be a <Buffer>. When the
6999	object is disposed, the directory and its contents will be removed if it still
7000	exists. If the directory cannot be deleted, disposal will throw an error. The
7001	object has a remove() method which will perform the same task.
7002	See the MDN documentation on using statements for more information about
7003	explicit resource management.
7004	For detailed information, see the documentation of fs.mkdtemp().
7005	There is no callback-based version of this API because it is designed for use
7006	with the using syntax.
7007	The optional options argument can be a string specifying an encoding, or an
7008	object with an encoding property specifying the character encoding to use.

7108	fs.readFileSync(path[, options])#
7109	
7110	History
7111	
7112	VersionChanges
7113	v24.19.0
7114	Added support for the buffer option.
7115	v7.6.0
7116	The path parameter can be a WHATWG URL object using file: protocol.
7117	v5.0.0
7118	The path parameter can be a file descriptor now.
7119	v0.1.8
7120	Added in: v0.1.8
7121	
7122	
7123	
7124	
7125	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> | <integer> filename or file descriptor
7126	options <Object> | <string>
7127	
7128	encoding <string> | <null> Default: null
7129	flag <string> See support of file system flags. Default: 'r'.
7130	buffer <Buffer> | <TypedArray> | <DataView> | <Function> A buffer to read into, or a
7131	function called with the file size that returns the buffer.
7132	
7133	
7134	Returns: <string> | <Buffer>
7135	
7136	Returns the contents of the path.
7137	For detailed information, see the documentation of the asynchronous version of
7138	this API: fs.readFile().
7139	If the encoding option is specified then this function returns a
7140	string. Otherwise it returns a buffer.
7141	If buffer is provided and no encoding is specified, the returned <Buffer> is
7142	a view over the supplied buffer containing only the bytes read. If the
7143	supplied buffer is too small to contain the entire file, an error will be
7144	thrown.
7145	Similar to fs.readFile(), when the path is a directory, the behavior of
7146	fs.readFileSync() is platform-specific.
7147	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
7148	
7149	// macOS, Linux, and Windows
7150	readFileSync('<directory>');
7151	// => [Error: EISDIR: illegal operation on a directory, read <directory>]
7152	
7153	// FreeBSD
7154	readFileSync('<directory>'); // => <data> copy
7155

7326	fs.rmdirSync(path[, options])#
7327	
7328	History
7329	
7330	VersionChanges
7331	v16.0.0
7332	Using fs.rmdirSync(path, { recursive: true }) on a path that is a file is no longer permitted and results in an ENOENT error on Windows and an ENOTDIR error on POSIX.
7333	v16.0.0
7334	Using fs.rmdirSync(path, { recursive: true }) on a path that does not exist is no longer permitted and results in a ENOENT error.
7335	v16.0.0
7336	The recursive option is deprecated, using it triggers a deprecation warning.
7337	v14.14.0
7338	The recursive option is deprecated, use fs.rmSync instead.
7339	v13.3.0, v12.16.0
7340	The maxBusyTries option is renamed to maxRetries, and its default is 0. The emfileWait option has been removed, and EMFILE errors use the same retry logic as other errors. The retryDelay option is now supported. ENFILE errors are now retried.
7341	v12.10.0
7342	The recursive, maxBusyTries, and emfileWait options are now supported.
7343	v7.6.0
7344	The path parameters can be a WHATWG URL object using file: protocol.
7345	v0.1.21
7346	Added in: v0.1.21
7347	
7348	
7349	
7350	
7351	path <string> | <Buffer> | <URL>
7352	options <Object>
7353	
7354	maxRetries <integer> If an EBUSY, EMFILE, ENFILE, ENOTEMPTY, or
7355	EPERM error is encountered, Node.js retries the operation with a linear
7356	backoff wait of retryDelay milliseconds longer on each try. This option
7357	represents the number of retries. This option is ignored if the recursive
7358	option is not true. Default: 0.
7359	recursive <boolean> If true, perform a recursive directory removal. In
7360	recursive mode, operations are retried on failure. Default: false.
7361	Deprecated.
7362	retryDelay <integer> The amount of time in milliseconds to wait between
7363	retries. This option is ignored if the recursive option is not true.
7364	Default: 100.
7365	
7366	
7367	
7368	Synchronous rmdir(2). Returns undefined.
7369	Using fs.rmdirSync() on a file (not a directory) results in an ENOENT error
7370	on Windows and an ENOTDIR error on POSIX.
7371	To get a behavior similar to the rm -rf Unix command, use fs.rmSync()
7372	with options { recursive: true, force: true }.

7669	Class: fs.Dir#
7670	
7671	Added in: v12.12.0
7672	
7673	A class representing a directory stream.
7674	Created by fs.opendir(), fs.opendirSync(), or
7675	fsPromises.opendir().
7676	import { opendir } from 'node:fs/promises';
7677	
7678	try {
7679	const dir = await opendir('./');
7680	for await (const dirent of dir)
7681	console.log(dirent.name);
7682	} catch (err) {
7683	console.error(err);
7684	} copy
7685	When using the async iterator, the <fs.Dir> object will be automatically
7686	closed after the iterator exits.
7687	
7688	dir.close()#
7689	
7690	Added in: v12.12.0
7691	
7692	
7693	Returns: <Promise>
7694	
7695	Asynchronously close the directory's underlying resource handle.
7696	Subsequent reads will result in errors.
7697	A promise is returned that will be fulfilled after the resource has been
7698	closed.

7740	dir.read()#
7741	
7742	Added in: v12.12.0
7743	
7744	
7745	Returns: <Promise> Fulfills with a <fs.Dirent> | <null>
7746	
7747	Asynchronously read the next directory entry via readdir(3) as an
7748	<fs.Dirent>.
7749	A promise is returned that will be fulfilled with an <fs.Dirent>, or null
7750	if there are no more directory entries to read.
7751	For directory reads handled by the native file system, directory entries
7752	returned by this function are in no particular order as provided by the
7753	operating system's underlying directory mechanisms.
7754	Entries added or removed while iterating over the directory might not be
7755	included in the iteration results.
7756	
7757	dir.read(callback)#
7758	
7759	Added in: v12.12.0
7760	
7761	
7762	callback <Function>
7763	
7764	err <Error>
7765	dirent <fs.Dirent> | <null>
7766	
7767	
7768	
7769	Asynchronously read the next directory entry via readdir(3) as an
7770	<fs.Dirent>.
7771	After the read is completed, the callback will be called with an
7772	<fs.Dirent>, or null if there are no more directory entries to read.
7773	Directory entries returned by this function are in no particular order as
7774	provided by the operating system's underlying directory mechanisms.
7775	Entries added or removed while iterating over the directory might not be
7776	included in the iteration results.
7777	
7778	dir.readSync()#
7779	
7780	Added in: v12.12.0
7781	
7782	
7783	Returns: <fs.Dirent> | <null>
7784	
7785	Synchronously read the next directory entry as an <fs.Dirent>. See the
7786	POSIX readdir(3) documentation for more detail.
7787	If there are no more directory entries to read, null will be returned.
7788	Directory entries returned by this function are in no particular order as
7789	provided by the operating system's underlying directory mechanisms.
7790	Entries added or removed while iterating over the directory might not be
7791	included in the iteration results.
7792	
7793	dir[Symbol.asyncIterator]()#
7794	
7795	Added in: v12.12.0
7796	
7797	
7798	Returns: <AsyncIterator> An AsyncIterator of <fs.Dirent>
7799	
7800	Asynchronously iterates over the directory until all entries have
7801	been read. Refer to the POSIX readdir(3) documentation for more detail.
7802	Entries returned by the async iterator are always an <fs.Dirent>.
7803	The null case from dir.read() is handled internally.
7804	See <fs.Dir> for an example.
7805	Directory entries returned by this iterator are in no particular order as
7806	provided by the operating system's underlying directory mechanisms.
7807	Entries added or removed while iterating over the directory might not be
7808	included in the iteration results.
7809

7849	Class: fs.Dirent#
7850	
7851	Added in: v10.10.0
7852	
7853	A representation of a directory entry, which can be a file or a subdirectory
7854	within the directory, as returned by reading from an <fs.Dir>. The
7855	directory entry is a combination of the file name and file type pairs.
7856	Additionally, when fs.readdir() or fs.readdirSync() is called with
7857	the withFileTypes option set to true, the resulting array is filled with
7858	<fs.Dirent> objects, rather than strings or <Buffer>s.
7859	When a directory is read, such as with fs.readdir() or
7860	fs.opendir(), the file type of each entry is the type reported by the
7861	operating system and may depend on the file system; for example, some file
7862	systems may report a type that differs from what fs.lstat() returns.
7863	Node.js calls fs.lstat() on such an entry only when the reported type
7864	is unknown. Use fs.lstat() when an accurate file type is required.
7865	
7866	dirent.isBlockDevice()#

7873	Returns true if the <fs.Dirent> object describes a block device.

7959	Class: fs.FSWatcher#
7960	
7961	Added in: v0.5.8
7962	
7963	
7964	Extends <EventEmitter>
7965	
7966	A successful call to fs.watch() method will return a new <fs.FSWatcher>
7967	object.
7968	All <fs.FSWatcher> objects emit a 'change' event whenever a specific watched
7969	file is modified.
7970	
7971	Event: 'change'#
7972	
7973	Added in: v0.5.8
7974	
7975	
7976	eventType <string> The type of change event that has occurred
7977	filename <string> | <Buffer> The filename that changed (if relevant/available)
7978	
7979	Emitted when something changes in a watched directory or file.
7980	See more details in fs.watch().
7981	The filename argument may not be provided depending on operating system
7982	support. If filename is provided, it will be provided as a <Buffer> if
7983	fs.watch() is called with its encoding option set to 'buffer', otherwise
7984	filename will be a UTF-8 string.
7985	import { watch } from 'node:fs';
7986	// Example when handled through fs.watch() listener
7987	watch('./tmp', { encoding: 'buffer' }, (eventType, filename) => {
7988	if (filename) {
7989	console.log(filename);
7990	// Prints: <Buffer ...>
7991	}
7992	}); copy
7993	
7994	Event: 'close'#
7995	
7996	Added in: v10.0.0
7997	
7998	Emitted when the watcher stops watching for changes. The closed
7999	<fs.FSWatcher> object is no longer usable in the event handler.
8000	
8001	Event: 'error'#
8002	
8003	Added in: v0.5.8
8004	
8005	
8006	error <Error>
8007	
8008	Emitted when an error occurs while watching the file. The errored
8009	<fs.FSWatcher> object is no longer usable in the event handler.
8010	
8011	watcher.close()#
8012	
8013	Added in: v0.5.8
8014	
8015	Stop watching for changes on the given <fs.FSWatcher>. Once stopped, the
8016	<fs.FSWatcher> object is no longer usable.
8017	
8018	watcher.ref()#
8019	
8020	Added in: v14.3.0, v12.20.0
8021	
8022	
8023	Returns: <fs.FSWatcher>
8024	
8025	When called, requests that the Node.js event loop not exit so long as the
8026	<fs.FSWatcher> is active. Calling watcher.ref() multiple times will have
8027	no effect.
8028	By default, all <fs.FSWatcher> objects are "ref'ed", making it normally
8029	unnecessary to call watcher.ref() unless watcher.unref() had been
8030	called previously.
8031	
8032	watcher.unref()#
8033	
8034	Added in: v14.3.0, v12.20.0
8035	
8036	
8037	Returns: <fs.FSWatcher>
8038	
8039	When called, the active <fs.FSWatcher> object will not require the Node.js
8040	event loop to remain active. If there is no other activity keeping the
8041	event loop running, the process may exit before the <fs.FSWatcher> object's
8042	callback is invoked. Calling watcher.unref() multiple times will have
8043	no effect.
8044	
8045	Class: fs.StatWatcher#
8046	
8047	Added in: v14.3.0, v12.20.0
8048	
8049	
8050	Extends <EventEmitter>
8051	
8052	A successful call to fs.watchFile() method will return a new <fs.StatWatcher>
8053	object.
8054	
8055	watcher.ref()#
8056	
8057	Added in: v14.3.0, v12.20.0
8058	
8059	
8060	Returns: <fs.StatWatcher>
8061	
8062	When called, requests that the Node.js event loop not exit so long as the
8063	<fs.StatWatcher> is active. Calling watcher.ref() multiple times will have
8064	no effect.
8065	By default, all <fs.StatWatcher> objects are "ref'ed", making it normally
8066	unnecessary to call watcher.ref() unless watcher.unref() had been
8067	called previously.
8068	
8069	watcher.unref()#
8070	
8071	Added in: v14.3.0, v12.20.0
8072	
8073	
8074	Returns: <fs.StatWatcher>
8075	
8076	When called, the active <fs.StatWatcher> object will not require the Node.js
8077	event loop to remain active. If there is no other activity keeping the
8078	event loop running, the process may exit before the <fs.StatWatcher> object's
8079	callback is invoked. Calling watcher.unref() multiple times will have
8080	no effect.

8146	Class: fs.Stats#
8147	
8148	History

8153	v8.1.0

8155	v0.1.21
8156	Added in: v0.1.21
8157	
8158	
8159	
8160	A <fs.Stats> object provides information about a file.
8161	Objects returned from fs.stat(), fs.lstat(), fs.fstat(), and
8162	their synchronous counterparts are of this type.
8163	If bigint in the options passed to those methods is true, the numeric values
8164	will be bigint instead of number, and the object will contain additional
8165	nanosecond-precision properties suffixed with Ns.
8166	Stat objects are not to be created directly using the new keyword.
8167	Stats {
8168	dev: 2114,
8169	ino: 48064969,
8170	mode: 33188,
8171	nlink: 1,
8172	uid: 85,
8173	gid: 100,
8174	rdev: 0,
8175	size: 527,
8176	blksize: 4096,
8177	blocks: 8,
8178	atimeMs: 1318289051000.1,
8179	mtimeMs: 1318289051000.1,
8180	ctimeMs: 1318289051000.1,
8181	birthtimeMs: 1318289051000.1,
8182	atime: Mon, 10 Oct 2011 23:24:11 GMT,
8183	mtime: Mon, 10 Oct 2011 23:24:11 GMT,
8184	ctime: Mon, 10 Oct 2011 23:24:11 GMT,
8185	birthtime: Mon, 10 Oct 2011 23:24:11 GMT } copy

8189	ino: 48064969n,
8190	mode: 33188n,
8191	nlink: 1n,
8192	uid: 85n,
8193	gid: 100n,
8194	rdev: 0n,
8195	size: 527n,
8196	blksize: 4096n,
8197	blocks: 8n,
8198	atimeMs: 1318289051000n,
8199	mtimeMs: 1318289051000n,
8200	ctimeMs: 1318289051000n,
8201	birthtimeMs: 1318289051000n,

8203	mtimeNs: 1318289051000000000n,
8204	ctimeNs: 1318289051000000000n,
8205	birthtimeNs: 1318289051000000000n,
8206	atime: Mon, 10 Oct 2011 23:24:11 GMT,
8207	mtime: Mon, 10 Oct 2011 23:24:11 GMT,

8457	stats.birthtime#
8458	
8459	Added in: v0.11.13
8460	
8461	
8462	Type: <Date>
8463	
8464	The timestamp indicating the creation time of this file.
8465	
8466	Stat time values#
8467	The atimeMs, mtimeMs, ctimeMs, birthtimeMs properties are
8468	numeric values that hold the corresponding times in milliseconds. Their
8469	precision is platform specific. When bigint: true is passed into the
8470	method that generates the object, the properties will be bigints,
8471	otherwise they will be numbers.
8472	The atimeNs, mtimeNs, ctimeNs, birthtimeNs properties are
8473	bigints that hold the corresponding times in nanoseconds. They are
8474	only present when bigint: true is passed into the method that generates
8475	the object. Their precision is platform specific.
8476	atime, mtime, ctime, and birthtime are
8477	Date object alternate representations of the various times. The
8478	Date and number values are not connected. Assigning a new number value, or
8479	mutating the Date value, will not be reflected in the corresponding alternate
8480	representation.
8481	The times in the stat object have the following semantics:
8482	
8483	atime "Access Time": Time when file data last accessed. Changed
8484	by the mknod(2), utimes(2), and read(2) system calls.
8485	mtime "Modified Time": Time when file data last modified.
8486	Changed by the mknod(2), utimes(2), and write(2) system calls.
8487	ctime "Change Time": Time when file status was last changed
8488	(inode data modification). Changed by the chmod(2), chown(2),
8489	link(2), mknod(2), rename(2), unlink(2), utimes(2),
8490	read(2), and write(2) system calls.
8491	birthtime "Birth Time": Time of file creation. Set once when the
8492	file is created. On file systems where birthtime is not available,
8493	this field may instead hold either the ctime or
8494	1970-01-01T00:00Z (ie, Unix epoch timestamp 0). This value may be greater
8495	than atime or mtime in this case. On Darwin and other FreeBSD variants,
8496	also set if the atime is explicitly set to an earlier value than the current
8497	birthtime using the utimes(2) system call.
8498	
8499	Prior to Node.js 0.12, the ctime held the birthtime on Windows systems. As
8500	of 0.12, ctime is not "creation time", and on Unix systems, it never was.
8501

8532	statfs.bavail#
8533	
8534	Added in: v19.6.0, v18.15.0
8535	
8536	
8537	Type: <number> | <bigint>
8538	
8539	Free blocks available to unprivileged users. Multiply by statfs.bsize
8540	to get the number of available bytes.
8541	
8542	import { statfs } from 'node:fs/promises';
8543	
8544	const stats = await statfs('/');
8545	const availableBytes = stats.bsize * stats.bavail;
8546	console.log(`Available space: ${availableBytes} bytes`);const { statfs } = require('node:fs/promises');
8547	
8548	(async () => {
8549	const stats = await statfs('/');
8550	const availableBytes = stats.bsize * stats.bavail;
8551	console.log(`Available space: ${availableBytes} bytes`);
8552	})();copy

8634	statfs.type#
8635	
8636	Added in: v19.6.0, v18.15.0
8637	
8638	
8639	Type: <number> | <bigint>
8640	
8641	Type of file system. A platform-specific numeric identifier for the type of
8642	file system. This value corresponds to the f_type field returned by
8643	statfs(2) on POSIX systems (for example, 0xEF53 for ext4 on Linux). Its
