8645	platforms.
8646	
8647	Class: fs.Utf8Stream#
8648	
8649	Added in: v24.6.0
8650	
8651	Stability: 1 - Experimental
8652	An optimized UTF-8 stream writer that allows for flushing all the internal
8653	buffering on demand. It handles EAGAIN errors correctly, allowing for
8654	customization, for example, by dropping content if the disk is busy.
8655	
8656	Event: 'close'#
8657	The 'close' event is emitted when the stream is fully closed.
8658	
8659	Event: 'drain'#
8660	The 'drain' event is emitted when the internal buffer has drained sufficiently
8661	to allow continued writing.
8662	
8663	Event: 'drop'#
8664	The 'drop' event is emitted when the maximal length is reached and that data
8665	will not be written. The data that was dropped is passed as the first argument
8666	to the event handler.
8667	
8668	Event: 'error'#
8669	The 'error' event is emitted when an error occurs.
8670	
8671	Event: 'finish'#
8672	The 'finish' event is emitted when the stream has been ended and all data has
8673	been flushed to the underlying file.
8674	
8675	Event: 'ready'#
8676	The 'ready' event is emitted when the stream is ready to accept writes.
8677	
8678	Event: 'write'#
8679	The 'write' event is emitted when a write operation has completed. The number
8680	of bytes written is passed as the first argument to the event handler.
8681	
8682	new fs.Utf8Stream([options])#
8683	
8684	options <Object>
8685	
8686	append: <boolean> Appends writes to dest file instead of truncating it.
8687	Default: true.
8688	contentMode: <string> Which type of data you can send to the write
8689	function, supported values are 'utf8' or 'buffer'. Default:
8690	'utf8'.
8691	dest: <string> A path to a file to be written to (mode controlled by the
8692	append option).
8693	fd: <number> A file descriptor, something that is returned by fs.open()
8694	or fs.openSync().
8695	fs: <Object> An object that has the same API as the fs module, useful
8696	for mocking, testing, or customizing the behavior of the stream.
8697	fsync: <boolean> Perform a fs.fsyncSync() every time a write is
8698	completed.
8699	maxLength: <number> The maximum length of the internal buffer. If a write
8700	operation would cause the buffer to exceed maxLength, the data written is
8701	dropped and a drop event is emitted with the dropped data
8702	maxWrite: <number> The maximum number of bytes that can be written;
8703	Default: 16384
8704	minLength: <number> The minimum length of the internal buffer that is
8705	required to be full before flushing.
8706	mkdir: <boolean> Ensure directory for dest file exists when true.
8707	Default: false.
8708	mode: <number> | <string> Specify the creating file mode (see fs.open()).
8709	periodicFlush: <number> Calls flush every periodicFlush milliseconds.
8710	retryEAGAIN <Function> A function that will be called when write(),
8711	writeSync(), or flushSync() encounters an EAGAIN or EBUSY error.
8712	If the return value is true the operation will be retried, otherwise it
8713	will bubble the error. The err is the error that caused this function to
8714	be called, writeBufferLen is the length of the buffer that was written,
8715	and remainingBufferLen is the length of the remaining buffer that the
8716	stream did not try to write.
8717	
8718	err <any> An error or null.
8719	writeBufferLen <number>
8720	remainingBufferLen: <number>
8721	
8722	
8723	sync: <boolean> Perform writes synchronously.
8724	
8725	
8726	
8727	
8728	utf8Stream.append#
8729	
8730	<boolean> Whether the stream is appending to the file or truncating it.
8731	
8732	
8733	utf8Stream.contentMode#
8734	
8735	<string> The type of data that can be written to the stream. Supported
8736	values are 'utf8' or 'buffer'. Default: 'utf8'.
8737	
8738	
8739	utf8Stream.destroy()#
8740	Close the stream immediately, without flushing the internal buffer.
8741	
8742	utf8Stream.end()#
8743	Close the stream gracefully, flushing the internal buffer before closing.
8744	
8745	utf8Stream.fd#
8746	
8747	<number> The file descriptor that is being written to.
8748	
8749	
8750	utf8Stream.file#
8751	
8752	<string> The file that is being written to.
8753	
8754	
8755	utf8Stream.flush(callback)#
8756	
8757	callback <Function>
8758	
8759	err <Error> | <null> An error if the flush failed, otherwise null.
8760	
8761	
8762	
8763	Writes the current buffer to the file if a write was not in progress. Do
8764	nothing if minLength is zero or if it is already writing.
8765	
8766	utf8Stream.flushSync()#
8767	Flushes the buffered data synchronously. This is a costly operation.
8768	
8769	utf8Stream.fsync#
8770	
8771	<boolean> Whether the stream is performing a fs.fsyncSync() after every
8772	write operation.
8773	
8774	
8775	utf8Stream.maxLength#
8776	
8777	<number> The maximum length of the internal buffer. If a write
8778	operation would cause the buffer to exceed maxLength, the data written is
8779	dropped and a drop event is emitted with the dropped data.
8780	
8781	
8782	utf8Stream.minLength#
8783	
8784	<number> The minimum length of the internal buffer that is required to be
8785	full before flushing.
8786	
8787	
8788	utf8Stream.mkdir#
8789	
8790	<boolean> Whether the stream should ensure that the directory for the
8791	dest file exists. If true, it will create the directory if it does not
8792	exist. Default: false.
8793	
8794	
8795	utf8Stream.mode#
8796	
8797	<number> | <string> The mode of the file that is being written to.
8798	
8799	
8800	utf8Stream.periodicFlush#
8801	
8802	<number> The number of milliseconds between flushes. If set to 0, no
8803	periodic flushes will be performed.
8804	
8805	
8806	utf8Stream.reopen(file)#
8807	
8808	file: <string> | <Buffer> | <URL> A path to a file to be written to (mode
8809	controlled by the append option).
8810	
8811	Reopen the file in place, useful for log rotation.
8812	
8813	utf8Stream.sync#
8814	
8815	<boolean> Whether the stream is writing synchronously or asynchronously.
8816	
8817	
8818	utf8Stream.write(data)#
8819	
8820	data <string> | <Buffer> The data to write.
8821	Returns <boolean>
8822	
8823	When the options.contentMode is set to 'utf8' when the stream is created,
8824	the data argument must be a string. If the contentMode is set to 'buffer',
8825	the data argument must be a <Buffer>.
8826	
8827	utf8Stream.writing#
8828	
8829	<boolean> Whether the stream is currently writing data to the file.
8830	
8831	
8832	utf8Stream[Symbol.dispose]()#
8833	Calls utf8Stream.destroy().
8834	This method enables the stream to be used with using, which
8835	will automatically destroy the stream when the scope exits. For more
8836	information, see the MDN documentation on using statements.
8837	
8838	Class: fs.WriteStream#
8839	
8840	Added in: v0.1.93
8841	
8842	
8843	Extends <stream.Writable>
8844	
8845	Instances of <fs.WriteStream> cannot be constructed directly. They are created and
8846	returned using the fs.createWriteStream() function.
8847	
8848	Event: 'close'#
8849	
8850	Added in: v0.1.93
8851	
8852	Emitted when the <fs.WriteStream>'s underlying file descriptor has been closed.
8853	
8854	Event: 'open'#
8855	
8856	Added in: v0.1.93
8857	
8858	
8859	fd <integer> Integer file descriptor used by the <fs.WriteStream>.
8860	
8861	Emitted when the <fs.WriteStream>'s file is opened.
8862	
8863	Event: 'ready'#
8864	
8865	Added in: v9.11.0
8866	
8867	Emitted when the <fs.WriteStream> is ready to be used.
8868	Fires immediately after 'open'.
8869	
8870	writeStream.bytesWritten#
8871	
8872	Added in: v0.4.7
8873	
8874	The number of bytes written so far. Does not include data that is still queued
8875	for writing.
8876	
8877	writeStream.close([callback])#
8878	
8879	Added in: v0.9.4
8880	
8881	
8882	callback <Function>
8883	
8884	err <Error>
8885	
8886	
8887	
8888	Closes writeStream. Optionally accepts a
8889	callback that will be executed once the writeStream
8890	is closed.
8891	
8892	writeStream.path#
8893	
8894	Added in: v0.1.93
8895	
8896	The path to the file the stream is writing to as specified in the first
8897	argument to fs.createWriteStream(). If path is passed as a string, then
8898	writeStream.path will be a string. If path is passed as a <Buffer>, then
8899	writeStream.path will be a <Buffer>.
8900	
8901	writeStream.pending#
8902	
8903	Added in: v11.2.0
8904	
8905	
8906	Type: <boolean>
8907	
8908	This property is true if the underlying file has not been opened yet,
8909	i.e. before the 'ready' event is emitted.
8910	
8911	fs.constants#
8912	
8913	Type: <Object>
8914	
8915	Returns an object containing commonly used constants for file system
8916	operations.
8917	
8918	FS constants#
8919	The following constants are exported by fs.constants and fsPromises.constants.
8920	Not every constant will be available on every operating system;
8921	this is especially important for Windows, where many of the POSIX specific
8922	definitions are not available.
8923	For portable applications it is recommended to check for their presence
8924	before use.
8925	To use more than one constant, use the bitwise OR | operator.
8926	Example:
8927	import { open, constants } from 'node:fs';
8928	
8929	const {
8930	O_RDWR,
8931	O_CREAT,
8932	O_EXCL,
8933	} = constants;
8934	
8935	open('/path/to/my/file', O_RDWR | O_CREAT | O_EXCL, (err, fd) => {
8936	// ...
8937	}); copy
8938	
8939	File access constants#
8940	The following constants are meant for use as the mode parameter passed to
8941	fsPromises.access(), fs.access(), and fs.accessSync().
8942	
8943	
8944	Constant
8945	Description
8946	
8947	
8948	F_OK
8949	Flag indicating that the file is visible to the calling process.
8950	This is useful for determining if a file exists, but says nothing
8951	about rwx permissions. Default if no mode is specified.
8952	
8953	
8954	R_OK
8955	Flag indicating that the file can be read by the calling process.
8956	
8957	
8958	W_OK
8959	Flag indicating that the file can be written by the calling
8960	process.
8961	
8962	
8963	X_OK
8964	Flag indicating that the file can be executed by the calling
8965	process. This has no effect on Windows
8966	(will behave like fs.constants.F_OK).
8967	
8968	
8969	The definitions are also available on Windows.
8970	
8971	File copy constants#
8972	The following constants are meant for use with fs.copyFile().
8973	
8974	
8975	Constant
8976	Description
8977	
8978	
8979	COPYFILE_EXCL
8980	If present, the copy operation will fail with an error if the
8981	destination path already exists.
8982	
8983	
8984	COPYFILE_FICLONE
8985	If present, the copy operation will attempt to create a
8986	copy-on-write reflink. If the underlying platform does not support
8987	copy-on-write, then a fallback copy mechanism is used.
8988	
8989	
8990	COPYFILE_FICLONE_FORCE
8991	If present, the copy operation will attempt to create a
8992	copy-on-write reflink. If the underlying platform does not support
8993	copy-on-write, then the operation will fail with an error.
8994	
8995	
8996	The definitions are also available on Windows.

8998	File open constants#
8999	The following constants are meant for use with fs.open().
9000	
9001	
9002	Constant
9003	Description
9004	
9005	
9006	O_RDONLY
9007	Flag indicating to open a file for read-only access.
9008	
9009	
9010	O_WRONLY
9011	Flag indicating to open a file for write-only access.
9012	
9013	
9014	O_RDWR
9015	Flag indicating to open a file for read-write access.
9016	
9017	
9018	O_CREAT
9019	Flag indicating to create the file if it does not already exist.
9020	
9021	
9022	O_EXCL
9023	Flag indicating that opening a file should fail if the
9024	O_CREAT flag is set and the file already exists.
9025	
9026	
9027	O_NOCTTY
9028	Flag indicating that if path identifies a terminal device, opening the
9029	path shall not cause that terminal to become the controlling terminal for
9030	the process (if the process does not already have one).
9031	
9032	
9033	O_TRUNC
9034	Flag indicating that if the file exists and is a regular file, and the
9035	file is opened successfully for write access, its length shall be truncated
9036	to zero.
9037	
9038	
9039	O_APPEND
9040	Flag indicating that data will be appended to the end of the file.
9041	
9042	
9043	O_DIRECTORY
9044	Flag indicating that the open should fail if the path is not a
9045	directory.
9046	
9047	
9048	O_NOATIME
9049	Flag indicating reading accesses to the file system will no longer
9050	result in an update to the atime information associated with
9051	the file. This flag is available on Linux operating systems only.
9052	
9053	
9054	O_NOFOLLOW
9055	Flag indicating that the open should fail if the path is a symbolic
9056	link.
9057	
9058	
9059	O_SYNC
9060	Flag indicating that the file is opened for synchronized I/O with write
9061	operations waiting for file integrity.
9062	
9063	
9064	O_DSYNC
9065	Flag indicating that the file is opened for synchronized I/O with write
9066	operations waiting for data integrity.
9067	
9068	
9069	O_SYMLINK
9070	Flag indicating to open the symbolic link itself rather than the
9071	resource it is pointing to.
9072	
9073	
9074	O_DIRECT
9075	When set, an attempt will be made to minimize caching effects of file
9076	I/O.
9077	
9078	
9079	O_NONBLOCK
9080	Flag indicating to open the file in nonblocking mode when possible.
9081	
9082	
9083	UV_FS_O_FILEMAP
9084	When set, a memory file mapping is used to access the file. This flag
9085	is available on Windows operating systems only. On other operating systems,
9086	this flag is ignored.
9087	
9088	
9089	UV_FS_O_TEMPORARY
9090	When set, the file is deleted automatically when the last handle to it
9091	is closed. This flag is available on Windows operating systems only. On
9092	other operating systems, this flag is ignored.
9093	
9094	
9095	UV_FS_O_SHORT_LIVED
9096	Hint that the file is short-lived, so the system avoids flushing it to
9097	disk when possible. This flag is available on Windows operating systems
9098	only. On other operating systems, this flag is ignored.
9099	
9100	
9101	UV_FS_O_SEQUENTIAL
9102	Hint that the file is accessed sequentially from beginning to end, to
9103	optimize caching. This flag is available on Windows operating systems only.
9104	On other operating systems, this flag is ignored.
9105	
9106	
9107	UV_FS_O_RANDOM
9108	Hint that the file is accessed randomly, to optimize caching. This flag
9109	is available on Windows operating systems only. On other operating systems,
9110	this flag is ignored.
9111	
9112	
9113	On Windows, only O_APPEND, O_CREAT, O_EXCL, O_RDONLY, O_RDWR,
9114	O_TRUNC, O_WRONLY, UV_FS_O_FILEMAP, UV_FS_O_TEMPORARY,
9115	UV_FS_O_SHORT_LIVED, UV_FS_O_SEQUENTIAL, and UV_FS_O_RANDOM are
9116	available.

9220	
9221	Notes#
9222	
9223	Ordering of callback and promise-based operations#
9224	Because they are executed asynchronously by the underlying thread pool,
9225	there is no guaranteed ordering when using either the callback or
9226	promise-based methods.
9227	For example, the following is prone to error because the fs.stat()
9228	operation might complete before the fs.rename() operation:
9229	const fs = require('node:fs');
9230	
9231	fs.rename('/tmp/hello', '/tmp/world', (err) => {
9232	if (err) throw err;
9233	console.log('renamed complete');
9234	});
9235	fs.stat('/tmp/world', (err, stats) => {
9236	if (err) throw err;
9237	console.log(`stats: ${JSON.stringify(stats)}`);
9238	}); copy
9239	It is important to correctly order the operations by awaiting the results
9240	of one before invoking the other:
9241	
9242	import { rename, stat } from 'node:fs/promises';
9243	
9244	const oldPath = '/tmp/hello';
9245	const newPath = '/tmp/world';
9246	
9247	try {
9248	await rename(oldPath, newPath);
9249	const stats = await stat(newPath);
9250	console.log(`stats: ${JSON.stringify(stats)}`);
9251	} catch (error) {
9252	console.error('there was an error:', error.message);
9253	}const { rename, stat } = require('node:fs/promises');
9254	
9255	(async function(oldPath, newPath) {
9256	try {
9257	await rename(oldPath, newPath);
9258	const stats = await stat(newPath);
9259	console.log(`stats: ${JSON.stringify(stats)}`);
9260	} catch (error) {
9261	console.error('there was an error:', error.message);
9262	}
9263	})('/tmp/hello', '/tmp/world');copy
9264	Or, when using the callback APIs, move the fs.stat() call into the callback
9265	of the fs.rename() operation:
9266	
9267	import { rename, stat } from 'node:fs';
9268	
9269	rename('/tmp/hello', '/tmp/world', (err) => {
9270	if (err) throw err;
9271	stat('/tmp/world', (err, stats) => {
9272	if (err) throw err;
9273	console.log(`stats: ${JSON.stringify(stats)}`);
9274	});
9275	});const { rename, stat } = require('node:fs');
9276	
9277	rename('/tmp/hello', '/tmp/world', (err) => {
9278	if (err) throw err;
9279	stat('/tmp/world', (err, stats) => {
9280	if (err) throw err;
9281	console.log(`stats: ${JSON.stringify(stats)}`);
9282	});
9283	});copy
9284	
9285	File paths#
9286	Most fs operations accept file paths that may be specified in the form of
9287	a string, a <Buffer>, or a <URL> object using the file: protocol.
9288	
9289	String paths#
9290	String paths are interpreted as UTF-8 character sequences identifying
9291	the absolute or relative filename. Relative paths will be resolved relative
9292	to the current working directory as determined by calling process.cwd().
9293	Example using an absolute path on POSIX:
9294	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
9295	
9296	let fd;
9297	try {
9298	fd = await open('/open/some/file.txt', 'r');
9299	// Do something with the file
9300	} finally {
9301	await fd?.close();
9302	} copy
9303	Example using a relative path on POSIX (relative to process.cwd()):
9304	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
9305	
9306	let fd;
9307	try {
9308	fd = await open('file.txt', 'r');
9309	// Do something with the file
9310	} finally {
9311	await fd?.close();
9312	} copy
9313	
9314	File URL paths#
9315	
9316	Added in: v7.6.0
9317	
9318	For most node:fs module functions, the path or filename argument may be
9319	passed as a <URL> object using the file: protocol.
9320	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
9321	
9322	readFileSync(new URL('file:///tmp/hello')); copy
9323	file: URLs are always absolute paths.
9324	
9325	Platform-specific considerations#
9326	On Windows, file: <URL>s with a host name convert to UNC paths, while file:
9327	<URL>s with drive letters convert to local absolute paths. file: <URL>s
9328	with no host name and no drive letter will result in an error:
9329	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
9330	// On Windows :
9331	
9332	// - WHATWG file URLs with hostname convert to UNC path
9333	// file://hostname/p/a/t/h/file => \\hostname\p\a\t\h\file
9334	readFileSync(new URL('file://hostname/p/a/t/h/file'));
9335	
9336	// - WHATWG file URLs with drive letters convert to absolute path
9337	// file:///C:/tmp/hello => C:\tmp\hello
9338	readFileSync(new URL('file:///C:/tmp/hello'));
9339	
9340	// - WHATWG file URLs without hostname must have a drive letters
9341	readFileSync(new URL('file:///notdriveletter/p/a/t/h/file'));
9342	readFileSync(new URL('file:///c/p/a/t/h/file'));
9343	// TypeError [ERR_INVALID_FILE_URL_PATH]: File URL path must be absolute copy
9344	file: <URL>s with drive letters must use : as a separator just after
9345	the drive letter. Using another separator will result in an error.
9346	On all other platforms, file: <URL>s with a host name are unsupported and
9347	will result in an error:
9348	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
9349	// On other platforms:
9350	
9351	// - WHATWG file URLs with hostname are unsupported
9352	// file://hostname/p/a/t/h/file => throw!
9353	readFileSync(new URL('file://hostname/p/a/t/h/file'));
9354	// TypeError [ERR_INVALID_FILE_URL_PATH]: must be absolute
9355	
9356	// - WHATWG file URLs convert to absolute path
9357	// file:///tmp/hello => /tmp/hello
9358	readFileSync(new URL('file:///tmp/hello')); copy
9359	A file: <URL> having encoded slash characters will result in an error on all
9360	platforms:
9361	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
9362	
9363	// On Windows
9364	readFileSync(new URL('file:///C:/p/a/t/h/%2F'));
9365	readFileSync(new URL('file:///C:/p/a/t/h/%2f'));
9366	/* TypeError [ERR_INVALID_FILE_URL_PATH]: File URL path must not include encoded
9367	\ or / characters */
9368	
9369	// On POSIX
9370	readFileSync(new URL('file:///p/a/t/h/%2F'));
9371	readFileSync(new URL('file:///p/a/t/h/%2f'));
9372	/* TypeError [ERR_INVALID_FILE_URL_PATH]: File URL path must not include encoded
9373	/ characters */ copy
9374	On Windows, file: <URL>s having encoded backslash will result in an error:
9375	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
9376	
9377	// On Windows
9378	readFileSync(new URL('file:///C:/path/%5C'));
9379	readFileSync(new URL('file:///C:/path/%5c'));
9380	/* TypeError [ERR_INVALID_FILE_URL_PATH]: File URL path must not include encoded
9381	\ or / characters */ copy
9382	
9383	Buffer paths#
9384	Paths specified using a <Buffer> are useful primarily on certain POSIX
9385	operating systems that treat file paths as opaque byte sequences. On such
9386	systems, it is possible for a single file path to contain sub-sequences that
9387	use multiple character encodings. As with string paths, <Buffer> paths may
9388	be relative or absolute:
9389	Example using an absolute path on POSIX:
9390	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
9391	import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer';
9392	
9393	let fd;
9394	try {
9395	fd = await open(Buffer.from('/open/some/file.txt'), 'r');
9396	// Do something with the file
9397	} finally {
9398	await fd?.close();
9399	} copy
9400	
9401	Per-drive working directories on Windows#
9402	On Windows, Node.js follows the concept of per-drive working directory. This
9403	behavior can be observed when using a drive path without a backslash. For
9404	example fs.readdirSync('C:\\') can potentially return a different result than
9405	fs.readdirSync('C:'). For more information, see
9406	this MSDN page.
9407	
9408	File descriptors#
9409	On POSIX systems, for every process, the kernel maintains a table of currently
9410	open files and resources. Each open file is assigned a simple numeric
9411	identifier called a file descriptor. At the system-level, all file system
9412	operations use these file descriptors to identify and track each specific
9413	file. Windows systems use a different but conceptually similar mechanism for
9414	tracking resources. To simplify things for users, Node.js abstracts away the
9415	differences between operating systems and assigns all open files a numeric file
9416	descriptor.
9417	The callback-based fs.open(), and synchronous fs.openSync() methods open a
9418	file and allocate a new file descriptor. Once allocated, the file descriptor may
9419	be used to read data from, write data to, or request information about the file.
9420	Operating systems limit the number of file descriptors that may be open
9421	at any given time so it is critical to close the descriptor when operations
9422	are completed. Failure to do so will result in a memory leak that will
9423	eventually cause an application to crash.
9424	import { open, close, fstat } from 'node:fs';
9425	
9426	function closeFd(fd) {
9427	close(fd, (err) => {
9428	if (err) throw err;
9429	});
9430	}
9431	
9432	open('/open/some/file.txt', 'r', (err, fd) => {
9433	if (err) throw err;
9434	try {
9435	fstat(fd, (err, stat) => {
9436	if (err) {
9437	closeFd(fd);
9438	throw err;
9439	}
9440	
9441	// use stat
9442	
9443	closeFd(fd);
9444	});
9445	} catch (err) {
9446	closeFd(fd);
9447	throw err;
9448	}
9449	}); copy
9450	The promise-based APIs use a <FileHandle> object in place of the numeric
9451	file descriptor. These objects are better managed by the system to ensure
9452	that resources are not leaked. However, it is still required that they are
9453	closed when operations are completed:
9454	import { open } from 'node:fs/promises';
9455	
9456	let file;
9457	try {
9458	file = await open('/open/some/file.txt', 'r');
9459	const stat = await file.stat();
9460	// use stat
9461	} finally {
9462	await file.close();
9463	} copy
9464	
9465	Threadpool usage#
9466	All callback and promise-based file system APIs (with the exception of
9467	fs.FSWatcher()) use libuv's threadpool. This can have surprising and negative
9468	performance implications for some applications. See the
9469	UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE documentation for more information.
9470	
9471	File system flags#
9472	The following flags are available wherever the flag option takes a
9473	string.
9474	
9475	
9476	'a': Open file for appending.
9477	The file is created if it does not exist.
9478	
9479	
9480	'ax': Like 'a' but fails if the path exists.
9481	
9482	
9483	'a+': Open file for reading and appending.
9484	The file is created if it does not exist.
9485	
9486	
9487	'ax+': Like 'a+' but fails if the path exists.
9488	
9489	
9490	'as': Open file for appending in synchronous mode.
9491	The file is created if it does not exist.
9492	
9493	
9494	'as+': Open file for reading and appending in synchronous mode.
9495	The file is created if it does not exist.
9496	
9497	
9498	'r': Open file for reading.
9499	An exception occurs if the file does not exist.
9500	
9501	
9502	'rs': Open file for reading in synchronous mode.
9503	An exception occurs if the file does not exist.
9504	
9505	
9506	'r+': Open file for reading and writing.
9507	An exception occurs if the file does not exist.
9508	
9509	
9510	'rs+': Open file for reading and writing in synchronous mode. Instructs
9511	the operating system to bypass the local file system cache.
9512	This is primarily useful for opening files on NFS mounts as it allows
9513	skipping the potentially stale local cache. It has a very real impact on
9514	I/O performance so using this flag is not recommended unless it is needed.
9515	This doesn't turn fs.open() or fsPromises.open() into a synchronous
9516	blocking call. If synchronous operation is desired, something like
9517	fs.openSync() should be used.
9518	
9519	
9520	'w': Open file for writing.
9521	The file is created (if it does not exist) or truncated (if it exists).
9522	
9523	
9524	'wx': Like 'w' but fails if the path exists.
9525	
9526	
9527	'w+': Open file for reading and writing.
9528	The file is created (if it does not exist) or truncated (if it exists).
9529	
9530	
9531	'wx+': Like 'w+' but fails if the path exists.
9532	
9533	
9534	flag can also be a number as documented by open(2); commonly used constants
9535	are available from fs.constants. On Windows, flags are translated to
9536	their equivalent ones where applicable, e.g. O_WRONLY to FILE_GENERIC_WRITE,
9537	or O_EXCL|O_CREAT to CREATE_NEW, as accepted by CreateFileW.
9538	The exclusive flag 'x' (O_EXCL flag in open(2)) causes the operation to
9539	return an error if the path already exists. On POSIX, if the path is a symbolic
9540	link, using O_EXCL returns an error even if the link is to a path that does
9541	not exist. The exclusive flag might not work with network file systems.
9542	On Linux, positional writes don't work when the file is opened in append mode.
9543	The kernel ignores the position argument and always appends the data to
9544	the end of the file.
9545	Modifying a file rather than replacing it may require the flag option to be
9546	set to 'r+' rather than the default 'w'.
9547	The behavior of some flags are platform-specific. As such, opening a directory
9548	on macOS and Linux with the 'a+' flag, as in the example below, will return an
9549	error. In contrast, on Windows and FreeBSD, a file descriptor or a FileHandle
9550	will be returned.
9551	// macOS and Linux
9552	fs.open('<directory>', 'a+', (err, fd) => {
9553	// => [Error: EISDIR: illegal operation on a directory, open <directory>]
9554	});
9555	
9556	// Windows and FreeBSD
9557	fs.open('<directory>', 'a+', (err, fd) => {
9558	// => null, <fd>
9559	}); copy
9560	On Windows, opening an existing hidden file using the 'w' flag (either
9561	through fs.open(), fs.writeFile(), or fsPromises.open()) will fail with
9562	EPERM. Existing hidden files can be opened for writing with the 'r+' flag.
9563	A call to fs.ftruncate() or filehandle.truncate() can be used to reset
9564	the file contents.
9565	
9566
